What are these people to me? I hate them all. They know it and are offended.

After dinner, put on my cap and rushed out anywhere to escape. Walked to the end of the street, not knowing where I was going or what doing. Stopped and stared with fixed eyes at the traffic in Kensington Road, undetermined what to do with myself and unable to make up my mind (volitional paralysis). Turned round, walked home, and went straight to bed 9 p.m., anxiously looking forward to tomorrow evening when I go to see her again, but at the same time wondering how on earth I am to get through tomorrow’s round before the evening comes.﻿ ﻿… This is a hand-to-mouth existence. My own inner life is scorching up all outside interests. Zoology appears as a curious thing in a Baghdad bazaar. I sit in my room at the B.M. and play with it; I let it trickle through my fingers and roll away like a child playing with quicksilver.

July 11

Over to the flat. She was looking beautiful in a black dress, with a white silk blouse, and a Byron collar, negligently open in front as if a button had come out. She said I varied: sometimes I went up in her estimation, sometimes down; once I went down very low. I understood her to say I was now **up**! Alleluia!

July 14

… It would take too long and I am too tired to write out all the varying phases of this day’s life﻿—all its impressions and petty miseries chasing one another across my consciousness or leapfrogging over my chest like gleeful fiends.8

July 21

===Chinese===

我什么工作也没做。……我一动不动坐在椅子里，两个大拇指敲着魔鬼的鼓点，一个钟头接一个钟头，在同一个可怕的圈子里想啊，想啊，想啊。我无法工作。我没有勇气。我垮了胆。

五点钟我"回家"——回寄宿舍——越发绝望。

今晚来吃晚饭的有两个老处女、一个德国青年（一个好色、叫嚣、没脑子的东西）、一个女打字员（据说磕药）、一个酒鬼（一个月闹一回——前几天夜里还是H———把他扛上楼安顿到床上的）、两个在科文特花园乐队拉琴的没骨头的提琴手、还有一位正跟坐我那一桌的男人闹卧室韵事的殖民地太太。这些人于我算什么？我恨他们全体。他们也知道，并且见怪了。

晚饭后我戴上帽子，冲出门去，随便去哪儿，只为逃开。走到街尾，不知要去哪里、要做什么。停下来，直勾勾盯着肯辛顿路上来往的车流，不知把自己怎么办好，也拿不定主意（意志瘫痪）。转过身，走回家，九点径直上床，一面急切盼着明晚再去看她的那一刻，一面又纳闷明天这一整天究竟怎么捱到天黑。……这是有一顿没一顿的日子。我自己的内心生活正把一切身外的兴味烧个精光。动物学在巴格达集市上显得是件稀奇玩意儿。我坐在大英博物馆我的房间里摆弄它；我让它从指缝间淌走、滚开，像个玩水银的孩子。

7月11日

到公寓去。她穿一身黑裙，配白绸上衣，拜伦式领口，前面漫不经心地敞着，像掉了颗扣子。她说我这人起伏不定：有时在她那儿升一升，有时降一降；有一回降得非常之低。我听明白了我现在是**升**着的！哈利路亚！

7月14日

……要把这一天里千变万化的各个阶段一一写出来，费时太久，我也太累——那些印象、那些鸡毛蒜皮的苦楚，在我意识里你追我赶，或像一伙欢天喜地的小鬼在我胸口跳背。8

7月21日

===Original===

Thoroughly enjoyed the journey up to town this morning. I secretly gloated over the fact that the train was dashing along over the rails to London bearing me and all the rest of the train’s company upon their pursuits﻿—wealth, fame, learning. I was inebriated with the speed, ferocity, and dash of living.﻿ ﻿… If the train had charged into the buffers I should have hung my head out of the window and cheered. If a man had got in my way, I’d have knocked him down. The wheels of the carriage were singing a lusty song in which I joined.

July 30

… We talked of men and women, and she said she thought men were neither angels nor devils but just men. I said I thought women were either angels or devils.

“I am afraid to ask you which you think me.”

“You needn’t,” I said shortly.

August 9

Horribly upset with news from home. Mother is really ill. The Doctor fears serious nerve trouble and says she will always be an invalid. This is awful, poor dear! It’s dreadful, and yet I have a tiny wish buried at the bottom of my heart that she may be removed early from us rather than linger in pain of body and mind. Especially do I hope she may not live to hear any grievous news of me.﻿ ﻿… What irony that she should lose the use of her right arm only two years after Dad’s death from paralysis. It is cruel for it reminds her of Dad’s illness.﻿ ﻿… What, too, would she think if she could have heard M﻿—﻿—’s first words to me yesterday on one of my periodical visits to his consulting room, “Well, how’s the *paralysis*?”

In the evening went over to see her. She was wearing a black silk gown and looked handsome.﻿ ﻿… She is always the same sombre, fascinating, lissom, soft-voiced She! She herself never changes.﻿ ﻿… What am I to do? I cannot give her up and yet I do not altogether wish to take her to my heart. It distresses me to know how to proceed. I am a wily fish.

August 10

Sat in the gardens with her. We sat facing the sun for a while until she was afraid of developing freckles and turned around, deliberately turning her back on good King Sol.﻿ ﻿… I said it was disrespectful.

“Oh! he doesn’t mind,” she said. “He’s a dear. He kissed me and said, ‘Turn round my dear if you like.’ ”

Isn’t she tantalising?

I wanted to say sarcastically, “I wonder you let him kiss you,” but there was a danger of the remark reviving the dead.

August 14

===Chinese===

今早进城的旅程让我大大享受。火车载着我和全车的人，在铁轨上朝伦敦风驰电掣，奔向各自的追求——财富、名声、学问，我暗暗得意。我陶醉于生活的速度、狠劲与冲劲。……倘若火车一头撞上缓冲器，我准会把头探出窗外喝彩。谁挡我的道，我就把他打倒。车轮唱着一支粗豪的歌，我也跟着唱。

7月30日

……我们谈男人和女人，她说她觉得男人既不是天使也不是魔鬼，就只是男人。我说我觉得女人要么是天使，要么是魔鬼。

"我不敢问你把我当哪种。"

"不必问，"我简短地说。

8月9日

家里来的消息把我搅得心乱如麻。母亲是真的病了。医生担心是严重的神经毛病，说她往后要一直当个病人了。这太可怕了，可怜的亲亲！可怕极了，可我心底埋着一星小小的愿望：宁可她早些从我们身边被接走，也别在身心的疼痛里拖磨。我尤其希望她不要活着听到关于我的什么凶信。……具有讽刺意味的是，父亲瘫痪去世才两年，她就失掉了右臂的功能。这太残忍了，因为这总让她想起父亲的病。……还有，倘若她听见M———昨天在我例行的诊室拜访中对我说的头一句话，她会怎么想——"怎么样，那个*瘫痪*好些没？"

傍晚去看她。她穿一件黑绸长裙，样子很俊。……她永远是那个阴郁、迷人、柔软、软声细语的"她"！她本人从不改变。……叫我怎么办？我放不下她，却又并不全然想把她搂进心里。如何前行，我茫然无措。我是一条滑头的鱼。

8月10日

同她坐在园子里。我们对着太阳坐了一会儿，直到她怕晒出雀斑，转过身去，故意把背对着好国王太阳。……我说这不恭敬。

"哦！他不介意的，"她说，"他可亲呢。他亲了亲我，说：'亲爱的，想转过去就转吧。'"

她这不是撩人吗？

我想讥一句"亏你肯让他亲"，可这话有把死灰复燃的危险。

8月14日

===Original===

I tried my best, I’ve sought every loophole of escape, but I am quite unable to avoid the melancholy fact that her thumbs are﻿—lamentable. I am genuinely upset about it for I like her. No one more than I would be more delighted if they were otherwise.﻿ ﻿… Poor dear! how I love her! That’s why I’m so concerned about her thumbs.

August 21

A wire from A﻿—﻿— came at 11:50 saying “Darling Mother passed peacefully away yesterday afternoon.”﻿ ﻿… Yesterday afternoon I was writing Zoology and all last night I slept soundly.﻿ ﻿… It was quite sudden. Caught the first train home.

August 23

The funeral.

August 31

Staying at the Hotel du Guesclin at Cancale near St. Malo with my dear A﻿—﻿—.

This flood of new experiences has knocked my diary habit out of gear. To be candid, I’ve forgotten all about myself. I’ve been too engrossed in living to stand the strain of setting down and in cold blood writing out all the things seen and heard. If I once began I should blow through these pages like a whirlwind.﻿ ﻿… But what a waste of time with M. le batelier waiting outside with his bisque to take us mackerel fishing!﻿ ﻿…

September 8

Returned to Southampton yesterday. Have spent the night at Okehampton in Devonshire en route for T﻿—﻿— Rectory. This morning we hatched the ridiculous idea of hiring two little Dartmoor ponies and riding out from the town. A﻿—﻿— rides fairly well though he has not been astride a beast for years. As for me, I cannot ride at all! Yet I had the idea that I could easily manage a pretty little pony with brown eyes and a long tail. On going out into the Inn yard, was horrified﻿—two horses saddled﻿—one a large traction beast.﻿ ﻿… I climbed on to the smaller one, walked him out of the yard and down the road in good style without accident. Once in the country, however, my animal, the fresher of the two, insisted on a smart trot which shook me up a good deal so that I hardly kept my seat. This eventually so annoyed the animal that it began to fidget and zigzag across the road﻿—no doubt preparing to break away at a stretch gallop when once it had rid itself of the incomprehensible pair of legs across its back.

I got off quickly and swapped horses with A﻿—﻿—.

===Chinese===

我尽了最大的努力，寻遍每一处逃路，可怎么也躲不开这个令人黯然的事实：她的拇指——实在糟糕。我为此真心难过，因为我喜欢她。倘若它们不是这样，没有人会比我更高兴。……可怜的亲亲！我多爱她！所以我才这样牵心她的拇指。

8月21日

A———的电报十一点五十送到："亲爱的母亲已于昨日下午安详离世。"……昨天下午我正在写动物学，昨夜我一觉睡得很沉。……事情很突然。赶上头一班火车回家。

8月23日

葬礼。

8月31日

同亲爱的A———住在圣马洛附近康卡勒的盖克兰旅馆。

这洪水般涌来的新鲜经历，把我的日记习惯冲坏了。说句实话，我把自己全忘了。我一直忙着活，实在经不住坐下来冷冰冰地笔录所见所闻的折腾。真要动起笔来，我会像一阵旋风刮过这些纸页。……可船夫先生正带着他的比斯凯渔船等在外面，要带我们去钓鲭鱼，哪有工夫浪费！……

9月8日

昨天回到南安普敦。去T———牧师宅的途中，在德文郡的奥克汉普顿过了一夜。今天早上我们冒出一个荒唐的主意：雇两匹小的达特穆尔矮种马，骑出城去。A———骑得还算像样，虽然他多年没沾过马背。至于我，我压根儿不会骑！可我还以为自己轻轻松松就能驾驭一匹棕色眼睛、长尾巴的漂亮小马。走进客栈院子一看，吓坏了——两匹上了鞍的马——其中一匹是拉车的大牲口。……我爬上小的那匹，风度翩翩地把它牵出院子、走上大路，没出事故。可一到乡下，我这匹性子较烈的非要快步小跑，颠得我七荤八素，差点坐不住。这最终惹恼了牲口，它开始烦躁不安，在路上一扭一拐地斜穿——无疑在盘算着什么时候把背上这双莫名其妙的腿甩掉，好撒开四蹄奔一程。

我赶紧下来，跟A———换了马。

===Original===

Walked him most of the way, while A﻿—﻿— cantered forward and back to cheer me on. Ultimately however this beast, too, got sick of walking and began to trot. For a time I stood this well and began to rise in my saddle quite nicely. After two miles, horrible soreness supervened, and I had to get off﻿—very carefully, with a funny feeling in my legs﻿—even looked down at them to assure myself they were not bandy! In doing so, the horse﻿—this traction monster﻿—stepped on my toe and I swore.

On nearing the village, L﻿—﻿— arrived, riding A﻿—﻿—’s animal and holding his sides for laughing at me as I crawled along holding the carthorse by the bridle. Got on again and rode into the Rectory grounds in fine style like a dashing cavalier, everyone jeering at me from the lawn.

September 28

Having lived on this planet now for the space of twenty-four years, I can claim with some cogency that I am qualified to express some sort of opinion about it. I therefore hereby record that I find myself in an absorbingly interesting place where I live, move and have my being, dominated by one monstrous feature above all others﻿—the mystery of it all! Everything is so astonishing, my own existence so incredible!

Nothing explains itself. Everyone is dumb. It is like walking about at a masqued Ball.﻿ ﻿… Even I myself am a mystery to me. How wonderful and frightening that is﻿—to feel yourself﻿—your innermost and most substantial possession to be a mystery, incomprehensible. I look at myself in the mirror and mock at myself. On some days I am to myself as strange and unfamiliar as a Pterodactyl. There is a certain grim humour in finding myself here possessed of a perfectly arbitrary arrangement of lineaments when I never asked to be here and never selected my own attributes. To the dignity of a human being it seems like a coarse practical joke.﻿ ﻿… My own freakish physique is certainly a joke.

October 4

In London Again

K﻿—﻿— comes in from her dancing class, nods to me, hugs her sister around the neck and says﻿—

“Oh! you dear thing, you’ve got a cold.”

“I shouldn’t do that,” I remark, green-eyed, “she’s in an awful wax tonight.”

She: “Oh! I don’t mind K﻿—﻿—!”

(Laughter!)

October 8

===Chinese===

大部分路我牵着它走，A———则前后小跑着给我打气。可到后来，这头牲口也走腻了，开始小跑。有一阵我还顶得住，鞍上一起一落，姿态还算不坏。两英里之后，可怕的酸痛发作了，我只好下马——下得极其小心，两条腿里有一种怪怪的感觉——还低头看了看，确认它们没有变弯！这么一低头，那马——这头拉车的巨兽——一脚踩上我的脚趾，我骂了一句。

快到村子时，L———骑着A———那匹来了，笑得直不起腰，看我牵着拉车马的笼头一路蹭。我重新上马，神气十足地骑进牧师宅的院子，活像一位叱咤的骑士，草坪上人人冲我起哄。

9月28日

在这颗行星上住了整二十四年之后，我可以颇有几分雄辩地声称，自己有资格对它发表一点意见了。因此我在此记下：我发现自己身处一个极其引人入胜的地方，我在其中生活、行动、存有，而主宰它的是一种压倒其余一切的怪物般的特征——这万物之谜！一切都如此惊人，我自己的存在如此不可思议！

没有什么东西自我解释。人人都是哑巴。就像在一场假面舞会上走来走去。……连我本人对我也是个谜。那是何等奇妙又骇人——感觉你自己——你内在最要紧的产业——竟是一个谜，不可理解。我照着镜子嘲弄自己。有些日子里，我于我自己就像一只翼指龙一样陌生。我从不曾请求来到这里，也从不曾自选过自己的品貌，却发现自己到了这里，顶着一套全然武断安排下的眉眼，这里面有一种冷面的幽默。对于人的尊严，这像一个粗鄙的恶作剧。……我自己这副怪胎体格，当然是个玩笑。

10月4日

又在伦敦

K———从舞蹈班回来，朝我点点头，搂住她妹妹的脖子说——

"哦！宝贝儿，你着凉了。"

"我可不去搂她，"我醋意森森地说，"她今晚火大着呢。"

她："哦！K———我不怕！"

（大笑！）

10月8日

===Original===

Heard a knock at the door last night, and, thinking it was R﻿—﻿—, I unbolted it and let in a tramp who at once asked God to bless me and crown all my sorrow with joy. An amiable fellow to be sure﻿—so I gave him some coppers and he at once repeated with wonderful fervour, “God bless you, sir.”

“I wish He would,” I answered, “I have a horrible cold.”

“Ah, I know, I gets it myself and the hinfluenza﻿—have you had that, sir?”

In ten minutes I should have told him all my personal history. But he was thirsting for a drink and went off quickly and left me with my heart unburdened. London is a lonely place.

---

Today journeyed to —﻿—﻿— where I gave evidence as an *expert* in Economic Entomology at the County Court in a case concerning damage to furniture by mites for which I am paid £8 8s. fee and expenses and travelled first class. What irony!9

October 11

I may be a weak, maundering, vacillating fool but I cannot help loving her on one day, being indifferent the next and on some occasions even disliking her.﻿ ﻿… Today she was charming, with a certain warm glossy perfection on her face and hair.﻿ ﻿… And she loves me﻿—I could swear it. “And when a woman woos﻿ ﻿…” etc. How difficult for a vain and lonely man to resist her. She tells me many times in many dainty ways that she loves me without so much as stopping her work to talk.

I wish I were permanently and irresistibly enamoured. I want a bouleversement.﻿ ﻿…

October 13

Went to see a Harley Street oculist about the sight of one eye, which has caused a lot of trouble and worry of late and continuously haunted me with the possibility of blindness. At times, I see men as trees walking and print becomes hopelessly blurred.

The Specialist however is reassuring. The eye is healthy﻿—no neuritis﻿—but the adjustment muscles have been thrown out of gear by the nervous troubles of last spring.

---

Was ever man more sorely tempted? Here am I lonely and uncomfortable in diggings with a heart like nascent oxygen.﻿ ﻿… Shall I? Yes, but.﻿ ﻿… And I have neither health nor wealth.

October 22

The British Museum Reading Room

I saw it for the first time today! Gadzooks!! This is the only fit ejaculation to express my amazement! It’s a pagan temple with the Gods in the middle and all around, various obscure dark figures prostrating themselves in worship.

===Chinese===

昨夜听见敲门声，以为是R———，拔了闩放进来一个流浪汉。他立刻求上帝保佑我，并以喜乐为我一切忧愁加冕。确实是个和善的家伙——我给了他几个铜子，他随即以惊人的热忱又说了一遍："上帝保佑你，先生。"

"但愿他保佑，"我答道，"我重感冒。"

"啊，我知道，我自己也老犯，还有那个流行性感冒——先生您得过没？"

再过十分钟，我就要把自己的生平统统讲给他听了。可他正渴着酒，很快走了，撇下我一颗没有卸下重负的心。伦敦是个孤独的地方。

---

今天去了———，在郡法院以经济昆虫学*专家*身份出庭作证，案子关于螨虫损坏家具。为此我收八镑八先令出庭费加差旅，坐的头等舱。多讽刺！9

10月11日

我也许是个软弱、絮叨、摇摆的傻瓜，可我禁不住今天爱她，明天冷淡，有时甚至厌烦。……今天她很迷人，脸上和头发上有一层温润的光泽。……而她爱我——我敢发誓。"女人既追起男人来……"云云。一个虚荣而孤独的男人，如何招架得住她。她用许多精巧的方式，一遍遍告诉我她爱我，连停下手中的活计来讲话都不必。

但愿我能永久地、不可抗拒地热恋一场。我要一场天翻地覆。……

10月13日

为一只眼的视力去看了哈利街的眼科专家，这眼近来惹了许多烦恼，不断拿失明的可能来缠我。有时，我看人如树木行走，印刷字糊得一塌糊涂。

专家的话倒让人宽心。眼睛是健康的——没有神经炎——只是调焦的肌肉被去年春天的神经毛病搞乱了套。

---

可曾有人受过这样的诱惑？我孤零零住在寄宿屋里，浑身不自在，揣着一颗如初生氧般的心。……要不要？要，可是……而我既无健康，又无钱财。

10月22日

大英博物馆阅览室

今天我头一回见到它！天哪！！要表达我的惊异，只有这一声惊叹可用！这是一座异教的庙宇，诸神居中环立，四周是各种幽暗的身影，伏地礼拜。

===Original===

For anyone who is not simply a Sheep or Cow or whose nervous organisation is a degree more sensitive than the village blacksmith’s, it is a besetting peril to his peace of mind to be constantly moving about an independent being, with loves and hates, and a separate identity among other separate identities, who prowl and prowl around like the hosts of Midian﻿—ready to snarl, fight, seize you, bore you, exasperate you, to arouse all your passions, call up all the worst from the depths where they have lain hidden.﻿ ﻿… A day spent among my fellows goads me to a frenzy by the evening. I am no longer fit for human companionship. People string me up to concert pitch. I develop suspicions of one that he is prying, of another that he patronises. Others make me horribly anxious to stand well in their eyes and horribly curious to know what they think of me. Others I hate and loathe﻿—for no particular reason. There is a man I am acquainted with concerning whom I know nothing at all. He may be Jew, Gentile, Socinian, Pre-adamite, Anabaptist, Rosicrucian﻿—I don’t know, and I don’t care, for I hate him. I should like to smash his face in. I don’t know why.﻿ ﻿… In the whole course of our tenuous acquaintance we have spoken scarce a dozen words to each other. Yet I should like to blow up his face with dynamite. If I had £200 a year private income I should be in wait for him tomorrow round a corner and land him one﻿—just to indicate my economic independence. He would call for the police and the policeman﻿—discerning creature﻿—on arrival, would surely say, “With a face like that, I’m not surprised.”

---

R﻿—﻿— said to me this morning, “Well, have you heard?” with an exuberance of curiosity that made my blood boil﻿—he was referring to my Essay still at the bar of the opinion of the Editor of the *English Review*. “You beast,” I snapped and walked off.

R﻿—﻿— shouted with laughter for he realizes my anger with him is only semi-serious: it is meant and not meant: meant, for it is justified by the facts; not meant, for I can’t be too serious over anything *au fond*.

---

===Chinese===

对任何一个不纯粹是绵羊或母牛、神经组织比村里铁匠敏感一分的人来说，整天在人群里走动都是对心境安宁的一种积习之祸：周围是一个个独立的存在，有爱有憎，各有各的身份，像米甸军一样逡巡不休——随时会龇牙、扑斗、攫住你、烦你、激怒你，唤起你全部的激情，把你最坏的东西从蛰伏的深处一一唤上来。……与同类厮混一天，到晚上我就被撩拨得近乎疯狂。我不再适合与人做伴。人们把我拧到演奏前的最高音。我疑心这个在窥探，那个在屈尊。另一些人害我发疯似的想博他们的好感，又发疯似的想知道他们怎么看我。还有一些我无缘无故地恨、厌恶。我认得一个人，关于他我一无所知。他可能是犹太人、外邦人、索齐尼派、前亚当派、再洗礼派、玫瑰十字会员——我不知道，也不在乎，因为我恨他。我想把他的脸捣烂。我不知道为什么。……在我们浅淡的相识里，我们俩说过的话拢共不到一打。可我想用炸药把他的脸崩上天。倘若我有每年两百镑的私人进项，明天我就埋伏在街角给他一拳——只为宣示我的经济独立。他会喊警察，而那位警察——有眼力的家伙——到场后准会说："长这么张脸，难怪。"

---

R———今早对我说："喂，听说了没有？"那股旺盛的好奇劲叫我血往上撞——他问的还是我那篇文章，仍押在《英国评论》主编的案头。"你这畜生，"我呵斥一声，走开了。

R———放声大笑，因为他明白我对他的恼火只有一半是当真：是当真，又不当真：当真，因为事实俱在；不当真，因为我对任何事情*骨子里*都没法太当真。

---

===Original===

Of all the grim and ridiculous odds and ends of chance that Fortune has rolled up to my feet, my friendship with a man like B﻿—﻿— is the grimmest and most ridiculous. He is a bachelor of sixty, rather good-looking, of powerful physique and a faultless constitution.﻿ ﻿… His ignorance is colossal and he once asked whether Australia, for example, though surrounded by water, is not connected up with other land underneath the sea. Being himself a child in intelligence (though commercially cunning), he has a great respect for my brains. Being himself a strong man, he views my ill-health with much contempt. His private opinion is that I am in consumption. When asked once by a lady if I were not going to be “a great man” one day, he replied, “Yes﻿—if he lives.” I ought to walk six miles a day, drink a bottle of stout with my dinner, and eat plenty of *onions*. His belief in the curative properties of onions is strong as death.﻿ ﻿…

His system of prophylaxis may be quickly summarised﻿—

-

Hot whisky *ad lib* and off to bed.

-

A woman.

These two sterling preventives he has often urged upon me at the same time tipping out a quantity of anathemas on doctors and physic.﻿ ﻿…

He is a cynic. He scoffs at the medical profession, the Law, the Church, the Press. Every man is guilty until he is proved innocent. The Premier is an unscrupulous character, the Bishop a salacious humbug. No doctor will cure, for it pays him to keep you ill. Every clergyman puts the Sunday-school teacher in the family way. His mouth is permanently distorted by cynicism.

He is vain and believes all women are in love with him. When playing the Gallant, he turns on a special voice, wears white spats, and looks like a Newmarket “Crook.”

“I lost my bus,” a girl says to him. “Lost your bust,” he answers, in broad Scotch. “I can’t see that you’ve done that.”﻿ ﻿… His sexual career has been a remarkable one, he claiming to have brought many women to bed, and actually to have lain with women of almost all European nationalities, for he has been a great traveller.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

命运女神滚到我脚边的所有那些狰狞而荒唐的碎骨零肉里，我跟B———这样一个人的交情要算最狰狞、最荒唐的一桩。他是个六十岁的单身汉，模样周正，体格强壮，体质无懈可击。……他的无知浩如烟海，譬如他问过，澳大利亚虽然四面环海，海底底下是不是还是跟别处陆地连着的。他自己在智力上是个孩子（虽然生意上精明），所以对我这副脑子颇为敬重。他自己是个壮汉，所以对我的病体颇为鄙夷。他私下的看法是我得了痨病。有位太太问他，我是不是有朝一日要成"大人物"，他答："是——只要他活得长。"我应当每天走六英里路，晚饭喝一瓶黑啤，多吃*洋葱*。他对洋葱疗效的信奉坚如死亡。……

他的防疫体系可以一言蔽之——

-

热威士忌随量，上床。

-

一个女人。

这两样万灵药他常一并劝我服用，同时把医生和汤药大卸八块地咒一遍。……

他是个愤世者。他讥笑医生这门行当、法律、教会、报界。人人有罪，除非证明清白。首相是个无耻之徒，主教是个好色的伪君子。没有医生肯治好你，留着你病着才于他有利。每个牧师都会把主日学教师肚子搞大。他的嘴被愤世嫉俗扭得永远歪着。

他虚荣，相信天下的女人都爱他。扮演殷勤男士时，他会切换出一副特制的嗓音，穿白鞋罩，模样像纽马克特的一个"骗子"。

"我误了班车，"一个姑娘对他说。"误了你的胸，"他用洪亮的苏格兰腔回答，"我看你可没误。"……他的性爱生涯堪称壮观，他自称上过许多女人的床，而且几乎同所有欧洲国家的女人都睡过，因为他是个大旅行家。……

===Original===

This man is my devoted friend!﻿ ﻿… And truth to tell I get on with him better than I do with most people. I like his gamey flavour, his utter absence of self-consciousness, and his doggy loyalty to myself﻿—his weaker brother. He may be depraved in his habits, coarse in his language, boorish in his manners, ludicrous in the wrongness of all his views. But I like him just because he is so hopeless. I get on with him because it is so impossible to reclaim him﻿—my missionary spirit is not intrigued. If he only dabbled in vice (for an experiment), if he had pale, watery ideas about current literature﻿—if﻿—to use his own favourite epithet﻿—he were genteel, I should quarrel.

October 30

Having developed a passion for a piece of sculpture by R. Boeltzig called the *Reifenwerferin*﻿—the most beautiful figure of a woman. I am already devoted to Rodin’s *Kiss* and have a photo of it framed in my bedroom. Have written to Bruciani’s.

I suspect that my growing appreciation of the plastic art is with me only distilled sensuality. I enjoy my morning bath for the same reason. My bath is a daily baptism. I revel in the pleasure of the pain of the cold water. I whistle gleefully because I am clean and cool and nude early in the morning with the sun still low, before the day has been stained by clothes, dirt, pain, exasperation, death.﻿ ﻿… How I love myself as I rub myself down!﻿—the cool, pink skin﻿—I could eat it! I want to be all day in a cold bath to enjoy the pain of mortifying the flesh﻿—it is so beautiful, so soft, so inscrutable﻿—if I cut out chunks of it, it would only bleed.

November 8

The other morning R﻿—﻿— said hyperbolically that he hadn’t slept all night for fear that, before he had time to put an arresting hand on my shoulder and say “Don’t,” I might have gone and become “Entangled.”﻿ ﻿…

… No, I’m as firm as a rock, my dear. But in imagination the affair was continued as follows﻿—

She: “I am fond of you, you know.”

He: “I wish you wouldn’t say these things to me﻿—they’re quite embarrassing.”

She: “Oh! my dear, I’m not serious, you know﻿—you’re such a vain young man.”

He: “Well, it’s equally embarrassing anyway.”

She: “Then I *am* serious.”

Tears.

I say: “I wish you would take me only for what I am﻿—a blackguard with no good intentions, yet no very evil ones﻿—but still a blackguard, whom you seem to find has engaging manners.”

===Chinese===

这个人却是我忠心的朋友！……说实在的，我跟他还处得比跟多数人都好。我喜欢他那股野味，他那份全然的没有自我意识，还有他对我——他这位较弱兄弟——狗一般的热诚。他也许习气下流，语言粗鄙，举止粗野，见解错得一塌糊涂到可笑的地步。可我喜欢他，正因为他无可救药。我跟她处得来，正因为改造他毫无指望——我的传教精神提不起兴致。倘若他只是浅尝一下罪恶（作为实验），倘若他对时下文学怀有几星苍白寡淡的见解——倘若——用他自己心爱的字眼说——他"上得了台面"，我们倒要吵架了。

10月30日

迷上了R. 贝尔齐格的一件雕塑《掷环女》——最美的女人体。我早已倾心于罗丹的《吻》，卧室里就镶着框挂着它的照片。已经写信给布鲁恰尼画店了。

我疑心我对造型艺术日增的鉴赏，不过是浓缩的肉欲。我享受晨浴也是同一个缘故。我的浴是每日一度的洗礼。我在冷水的痛楚之乐里畅快。清晨太阳还低的时候，我轻快地吹口哨，因为我干净、凉快、赤裸，这一天还没有被衣裳、污垢、疼痛、烦闷、死亡弄脏。……我给自己擦身时多爱自己啊！——凉凉的、粉红的皮肤——我真想咬它一口！我想整日泡在冷水里，享受苦修皮肉的痛楚——它是那样美，那样柔软，那样不可测——哪怕我剜下几块，它也只会流血。

11月8日

那天早上R———夸张地说，他一夜没睡，生怕来不及伸手按住我的肩膀说一声"别"，我就已经跑去"纠缠不清"了。……

……不，我稳如磐石，亲爱的。不过在想象里，这桩事是这样继续的——

她："你是知道的，我喜欢你。"

他："请你别对我说这些——怪难为情的。"

她："哦！亲爱的，我并不当真——你这人真爱虚荣。"

他："那也一样难为情。"

她："那我*就是*当真。"

眼泪。

我说："但愿你只按我的本相看我——一个没安好心的恶棍，倒也没有很坏的存心——总归是个恶棍，你却似乎觉得他举止讨人喜欢。"

===Original===

I breathe freely hoping to have escaped this terrible temptation and turn to go. But she, looking up smiling through a curtain of wet eyelashes, asks﻿—

“Won’t the blackguard stop a little longer?” In a moment my earth works, redoubts, and bastions fall down, I rush forward impetuously into her arms shouting, “I *will*, I *will*, I will as long as for eternity.”

(Curtain.)

---

I dramatised this little picture and much more last night before going to sleep when I was in a fever. I should succumb at once to the first really skilful coquette.

November 9

Ludo

We played Ludo together this evening and she won 2s. 6d. Handsomely gowned in black and wearing black ornaments, she sat with me in the lamplight on the sofa in the Morris Room, with the Ludo board between us placed on a large green cushion. Her face was white as parchment and her hair seemed an ebony black. I lolled in the opposite corner, a thin, elongated youth, with fair hair all stivered up, dressed in a light-brown lounge suit with a good trouser crease, a soft linen collar and﻿—a red tie! Between us, on its green cushion the Ludo board with its brilliantly coloured squares:﻿—all of it set before a background formed by the straight-backed, rectangular, settle-like sofa, with a charming covering which went with the rest of the scheme.

“Rather decorative,” —﻿—﻿— remarked in an audible voice, turning her head on one side and quizzing. I can well believe it was. *She* looked wholly admirable.

November 21

My Nightmare

Can’t get rid of my cough. I have so many things to do﻿—I am living in a fever of haste to get them done. Yet this cough hinders me. There is always something which drags me back from the achievement of my desires. It’s like a nightmare; I see myself struggling violently to escape from a monster which draws continuously nearer, until his shadow falls across my path, when I begin to run and find my legs tied, etc. The only difference is that mine is a nightmare from which I never wake up. The haven of successful accomplishment remains as far off as ever. Oh! make haste.

November 29

The *English Review* has returned my Essay!﻿—This is a keen disappointment to me. “I wish I could use this, but I am really too full,” the Editor writes. To be faintly encouraged and delicately rejected﻿—why I prefer the printed form.

December 1

More Irony

===Chinese===

我长出一口气，庆幸逃过了这个可怕的诱惑，转身要走。可她从一帘湿睫毛后面含笑仰起脸来问——

"这恶棍不肯多留一会儿吗？"转眼间，我的堑壕、棱堡、壁垒一齐塌了，我不管不顾冲进她怀里，喊道："我*留*，我*留*，我留到地老天荒。"

（幕落。）

---

昨夜发着热，睡前我把这幅小图景和更多的续篇搬演了一遍。头一个真正会调情的女人一来，我立刻就会缴械。

11月9日

鲁多棋

今晚我们一起玩鲁多棋，她赢了两先令六便士。她穿一身阔气的黑裙，戴黑色的饰物，在灯下同我坐在莫里斯室的长沙发上，中间的鲁多棋盘搁在一只大绿垫子上。她脸色白得像羊皮纸，头发黑得像乌木。我瘫在对面的角落里，一个瘦长条的年轻人，浅色的头发根根乍着，穿一身浅棕色便服西服，裤线笔挺，软亚麻领子，外加——一条红领带！我们中间，绿垫子上是那方鲁多棋盘，格子色彩鲜明：——这一切的背景，是那张直背、方正规整、状如高背长椅的沙发，罩着一幅与整套格局相配的迷人椅罩。

"挺有装饰味，"———侧过头，声音听得见地品评道。我完全信。*她*看上去再好看没有。

11月21日

我的噩梦

咳嗽总不见好。我有这么多事要做——我活在一种赶紧做完的高烧里。可这咳嗽拖着我。总有一样东西把我从愿望的达成上拽回来。就像一场噩梦：我看见自己拼命挣扎，要从一个不断逼近的怪物手里逃脱，直到它的影子罩上我的路，我开始奔跑，却发现双腿被捆住，等等。唯一的不同是，我这场噩梦永远醒不来。功成业就的港湾仍同从前一样遥远。哦！快点吧。

11月29日

《英国评论》退回了我的文章！——这于我是狠狠一记失望。"我真想用这篇，可惜版面实在太满，"主编写道。被轻轻地鼓励、又委婉地回绝——我倒宁愿要那张铅印的退稿单。

12月1日

又一重讽刺

===Original===

Renewed my cold﻿—I do nothing all day but blow my nose, cough, and curse Austin Harrison.

M﻿—﻿— thinks the lungs are all right. “There is nothing there, I think,” said he, this morning. Alleluia! I’ve had visions of consumption for weeks past and M﻿—﻿— himself has been expecting it. I always just escape: I always almost get something, do something, go somewhere, I have dabbled in a variety of diseases, but never got one downright10﻿—but only enough to make me feel horribly unfit and very miserable without the consolation of being able to regard myself as the heroic victim of some incurable disorder. Instead of being Stevenson with tuberculosis, I’ve only been Jones with dyspepsia. So, too, in other directions, big events have always just missed me: by Herculean efforts I succeeded in giving up newspaper journalism and breaking through that steel environment﻿—but only to become an Entomologist! I once achieved success in an Essay in the *Academy*, which attracted attention﻿—a debut, however, that never developed. I had not quite arrived. It is always *not quite*.

Yesterday, I received a state visit from the Editor of the *Furniture Record* seeking advice on how to eradicate mites from upholstering! I received him ironically﻿—but little did he understand.

I shot up like a ball on a bagatelle board all steamy into zoology (my once beloved science) but at once rolled dead into the very low hole of Economic Entomology! Curse.﻿ ﻿… Why can’t I either have a first-rate disease or be a first-rate zoologist?

Now just think what a much better figure I should have cut, from the artistic view point, had I remained a newspaper reporter who had taught himself prodigious embryology out of F. M. Balfour’s Textbook, who had cut sections of fowls’ eggs and newt embryos with a hand microtome, who had passionately dissected out the hidden, internal anatomy of a great variety of animals, who could recite Wiedersheim’s *Comparative Anatomy of Vertebrates* and patter off the difference between a nephridium and a coelomic duct without turning a hair﻿—or the phylogenetic history (how absorbing!) of the kidney﻿—pronephros, mesonephros and metanephros and all the ducts!﻿ ﻿… All this, over now and wasted. My hardly-won knowledge wrenched away is never brought into use﻿—it lies piled up in my brain rotting. I could have become a first-rate comparative anatomist.

December 3

===Chinese===

感冒又犯了——成天无所事事，光擤鼻涕、咳嗽、咒骂奥斯汀·哈里森。

M———认为肺没问题。"我想那儿什么都没有，"他今早说。哈利路亚！几个星期来我一直幻见痨病，连M———自己也一直在提防它。我总是差一点：我总是几乎得上什么、几乎做成什么、几乎去成什么地方；我在五花八门的病症里趟过水，可从没真正捞着一场10——只够让我觉得自己糟透了、惨透了，却又享受不到把自己当作某种不治之症的殉道英雄的安慰。我没当成害痨病的史蒂文森，只做成了害消化不良的琼斯。在其他方面也一样，大事件总是与我擦肩：我费了赫拉克勒斯般的力气，总算甩掉了新闻行当、冲破了那层铁壳——结果只落得当了昆虫学家！我有一回在《学院》上得了回文章的成功，引人注目——然而那次亮相再没了下文。我总差一步到位。永远都是*差一点*。

昨天，《家具记录》的主编亲临寒舍，登门求教如何消灭软垫里的螨虫！我带着讥诮接待了他——他哪里懂得。

我像弹子台上一颗球，一杆子打起来，直窜进动物学（我昔日心爱的科学）——却当场滚进了经济昆虫学那个最低的洞！该死。……为什么我不能要么得一场一流的病，要么当一名一流的动物学家？

想想吧，倘若我当年仍旧做报馆记者，靠鲍尔福的教科书自学了惊人的胚胎学，用手摇切片机切过鸡胚和蝾螈胚胎的切片，满怀激情地解剖过五花八门的动物的内在构造，能背出维德沙姆的《脊椎动物比较解剖学》，面不改色地顺口说出肾管与体腔管的区别——或者肾脏的系统发生史（多迷人！）——前肾、中肾、后肾和所有的导管！……那从艺术的眼光看，我会是一个体面得多的人物。……这一切，如今都完了、都浪费了。我辛辛苦苦赢得的知识被生生拧走，从未派上用场——它堆在我脑子里发烂。我本可以成为一名一流的解剖学家。

12月3日

===Original===

Cold better. So back at work﻿—gauging ale at Dunfermline as R﻿—﻿— puts it.

December 9

In the evening found it quite impossible to stay in the house any longer: some vague fear drove me out. I was alarmed to be alone or to be still. It is my cough, I think.

Had two glasses of port at the Kensington Hotel, conversed with the barmaid, and then came home.

December 10

“Don’t be an old fossil,” she said to me tonight, irrelevantly.

“Apropos of what?” I inquired.

“Mother, here’s W﻿—﻿— proposing to E﻿—﻿—! Do come,” cried —﻿—﻿—, with intent to confuse. I laughed heartlessly.

Dear, dear, where will it all end? It’s a sad business when you fall in love with a girl you don’t like.

December 26

Spent a romping day at the Flat. Kissed her sister twice under the mistletoe, and in the evening went to a cinema. After supper made a mock heroic speech and left hilarious.

1914

February 4

… Finally and in conclusion I have fallen ill again, have again resumed my periodical visits to the Doctor, and am swallowing his rat-poison in a blind faith as aforetime. In fact, I am in London, leading the same solitary life, seeing no one, talking to no one, and daily struggling with this demon of ill-health. Can no one exorcise him? The sight of *both* my eyes is affected now. Blindness?

B﻿—﻿— continues whoring, drinking, sneering. R﻿—﻿— as usual, devoid of emotion, cold, passionless, Shavian, and self-absorbed, still titillates his mind with etching, sociology, music, etc., and I have at last ceased to bore him with what he probably calls the febrile utterances of an overwrought mind.

Such is my world! Oh! I forgot﻿—on the floor below me is a corpse﻿—that of an old gentleman who passed away suddenly in the night. In the small hours, the landlady went for the Doctor over the way, but he refused to come, saying the old man was too aged. So the poor gentleman died alone﻿—in this rat hole of a place.

February 7

Intending to buy my usual 3d. packet of Goldflakes, entered a tobacconist’s in Piccadilly, but once inside surprised to find myself in a classy west-end establishment, which frightened my flabby nature into buying De Reszke’s instead. I hadn’t the courage to face the aristocrat behind the counter with a request for Goldflakes﻿—probably not stocked. What would he think of me? Besides, I shrank from letting him see I was not perfectly well-to-do.

February 14

===Chinese===

感冒好些。于是复工——用R———的话说，去丹弗姆林量啤酒去了。

12月9日

晚上发现实在没法再待在屋里：某种说不清的恐惧把我赶了出去。独处或静止都叫我惊惶。是我的咳嗽，我想。

在肯辛顿旅馆喝了两杯波尔图，同酒吧女侍聊了几句，然后回家。

12月10日

"别当个老古董，"她今晚没头没脑地对我说。

"此话怎讲？"我问。

"妈妈，W———在向E———求婚呢！快来，"———喊着，存心搅浑水。我无情地大笑。

天哪天哪，这一切要闹到哪里去？爱上一个自己不喜欢的姑娘，实在是桩伤心事。

12月26日

在公寓疯玩了一天。槲寄生底下吻了她妹妹两回，晚上去看了一场电影。夜宵后发表了一通装模作样的英雄演说，欢天喜地地走了。

1914年

2月4日

……最后，总结一句：我又病倒了，又恢复了去医生那里的周期拜访，又像从前那样盲目信仰地吞着他的耗子药。总之，我在伦敦，过着同样的孤独日子，谁也不见，谁也不理，天天同病魔缠斗。就没有人能把它赶走吗？如今我*两只*眼睛的视力都出了毛病。失明？

B———照旧嫖、喝、冷笑。R———照旧没有感情，冷冰冰、无激情，萧伯纳式，只顾自己，依旧拿蚀刻、社会学、音乐什么的撩拨他的头脑；而我终于不再拿——他多半称之为一个过分紧张的头脑的热昏话——去烦他了。

这就是我的世界！哦！忘了说——我楼下停着一具尸体——一位夜里猝然去世的老绅士。凌晨时分，女房东去请马路对面的医生，医生不肯来，说老人年纪太大。于是这位可怜的绅士孤零零地死了——死在这个耗子洞里。

2月7日

想买我往常那种三便士一包的"金片"烟，进了皮卡迪利的一家烟店，可一进门才发现是家高级的西区铺子，吓得我软塌塌的性子改买了德雷斯基牌。我没勇气对着柜台后那位贵族老爷开口要金片——多半没货。他会怎么看我？再说，我也不想让他看出我手头并不宽绰。

2月14日

===Original===

I wonder what this year has in store for me? The first twenty-four years of my life have hunted me up and down the keyboard﻿—I have been right to the top and also to the bottom﻿—very happy and very miserable. Yet I prefer the life that is a hunt and an adventure. I don’t really mind being chased like this. I almost thrive on the excitement. If I knew always where to look with any degree of certainty for my next day’s life I should yawn! “What if today be sweet,” I say, and never look ahead. To me, next week is next century.

The danger and uncertainty of my life make me cherish and hug closely to my heart various little projects that otherwise would seem unworthy. I work at them quickly, frantically, sometimes, afraid to whisper to a living soul what expectations I dare to harbour in my heart. What if *now* the end be near? Not a word! Let me go onward.

February 15

Today I have reviewed the situation carefully, exhaustively. I have peered into every aspect of my life and achievements and everything I have seen nauseates me. I can find no ray of comfort in anything I have done or in anything I might do. My life seems to have been a wilderness of futile endeavour. I started wrong from the very beginning. At the moment of my birth I was coming into the world in the wrong place and under wrong conditions. Why seek to overcome such colossal initial disadvantages? In this mood I found fault with my parentage, my inheritance, all my mental and physical disabilities.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

不知道这一年为我存着什么？我人生头二十四年，在琴键上把我上下追赶——我到过最高音，也到过最低音——极快乐，也极悲惨。可我偏爱这种追赶与冒险的人生。这样被追逐，我其实并不介意，几乎还靠这份刺激越发精神。倘若我总能有几分把握知道明天的日子在哪里，我准会打哈欠！"今日若甜，"我说，便从不往前看。于我，下星期就是下个世纪。

我生活的危险与无常，让我把种种小小的谋划珍重地搂在心口——换了别的时候，它们多半不值一顾。我做它们做得又快又疯，不敢向一个活人低声吐露我心里竟敢存着什么指望。倘若*此刻*大限已近，那又如何？一个字也别说！让我往前走。

2月15日

今天我把局面仔细地、彻底地检讨了一遍。我窥看了自己生活和成就的每一个方面，所见的一切都叫我作呕。在我做过的事、或将做的事里，我寻不出一线安慰。我这一生似乎是一片徒劳的荒漠。我从一开始就错了。我出生的那一刻，就在错误的地点、错误的境况下来到了这世界。何必妄想克服这么庞大的先天劣势？在这种情绪里，我挑剔起我的出身、我的遗产、我全部的心智与身体的缺陷。……

===Original===

This must be a form of incipient insanity. Even as a boy, I can remember being preternaturally absorbed in myself and preternaturally discontented. I was accustomed to exhaust my mind by the most harassing cross-examinations﻿—no Counsel at the Bar ever treated a witness more mercilessly. After a day of this sort of thing, when silently and morbidly in every spare moment, at meals, in school, or on a walk, I would incessantly ply the questions, “What is the ultimate value of your work, cui bono?” etc. I went to bed in the evening with a feeling of hopelessness and dissatisfaction﻿—haggard with considerations and reconsiderations of my outlook, my talent, my character, my future. In bed, I tossed from side to side, mentally exhausted with my efforts to obtain some satisfying conclusion﻿—always hopeful, determined to the last to be able to square up my little affairs before going to sleep. But out of this mazy, vertiginous mass of thinking no satisfaction ever came. *Now*, I thought﻿—or the *next moment*﻿—or as soon as I review and revise myself in this or in that aspect, I shall be content. And so I went on, tearing down and reforming, revising and reviewing, till finally from sheer exhaustion and very unhappy I fell asleep.

Next morning I was all right.

February 20

Am feeling very unwell. My ill-health, my isolation, baulked ambitions, and daily breadwinning all conspire to bring me down. The idea of a pistol and the end of it grows on me day by day.

February 21

After four days of the most profound depression of spirits, bitterness, self-distrust, despair, I emerged from the cloud today quite suddenly (probably the arsenic and strychnine begins to take effect) and walked up Exhibition Road with the intention of visiting the Science Museum Library so as to refer to Schafer’s *Essentials of Histology* (I have to watch myself carefully so that I may act *at once* as soon as the balance of mind is restored). In the lobby was a woman screaming as if in pain, with a passerby at her side saying sternly, “What is the matter with you?” as if she were making herself ridiculous by suffering pain in public.

===Chinese===

这准是初发疯癫的一种形式。还是个孩子时，我就记得自己异乎寻常地耽于自我，又异乎寻常地不知足。我惯于用最折磨人的交叉讯问耗尽自己的心思——法庭上没有哪个律师比这更不留情。这样过了一天——在学校的每顿饭、每次散步、每个空当里，都默不作声、病态地不断发问："你的工作究竟有什么终极价值，cui bono（于谁有益）？"等等——到了晚上，我便怀着无望与不满上床——为我的前程、才华、性格、未来翻来覆去、越想越憔悴。躺在床上，我翻来覆去，为求得一个满意的结论而心智耗竭——却总是怀着希望，决意到最后一刻也要在入睡前把小账结清。可从这一团迷宫般令人晕眩的思绪里，从来没有满意出来过。*现在*，我想——或者*下一刻*——或者等我从这个方面、那个方面把自己检讨修订一遍，我就满足了。就这样继续，拆了重建，修了再改，直到最后，纯粹因为力竭，我极不快活地睡着了。

第二天早上，我什么事也没有。

2月20日

身上很难受。病体、孤寂、受挫的抱负、每天糊口的操劳，联手把我往下拖。手枪和一了百了的念头，一天比一天长。

2月21日

在最深重的消沉、苦涩、自疑、绝望里泡了四天之后，今天我突然从云里钻了出来（大概是砒霜和马钱子碱开始见效），走上展览路，打算去科学博物馆图书馆查沙弗的《组织学纲要》（我得看紧自己，好等心神一恢复平衡就*立刻*行动）。门厅里有个女人在尖叫，像疼得厉害，旁边一个过路人厉声说："你这是怎么了？"仿佛她当众喊疼是自讨没趣。

===Original===

I passed by quickly, pretending not to notice lest﻿—after all﻿—I should be done out of my *Essentials of Histology*. Even in the Library I very nearly let the opportunity slide by picking up a book on squaring the circle, the preface and introduction of which I was forced to read.

March 4

The Entomological Society

There were a great many Scarabees present who exhibited to one another poor little pinned insects in collecting-boxes﻿ ﻿… It was really a one-man show, Prof. Poulton, a man of very considerable scientific attainments, being present, and shouting with a raucous voice in a way that must have scared some of the timid, unassuming collectors of our country’s butterflies and moths. Like a great powerful sheepdog, he got up and barked, “Mendelian characters,” or “Germ plasm,” what time the obedient flock ran together and bleated a pitiful applause. I suppose, having frequently heard these and similar phrases fall from the lips of the great man at these reunions, they have come to regard them as symbols of a ritual which they think it pious to accept without any question. So every time the Professor says, “Allelomorph,” or some such phrase, they cross themselves and never venture to ask him what the hell it is all about.

March 7

A Scots Fir

Have been feeling very “down” of late, but yesterday I saw a fine Scots Fir by the roadside﻿—tall, erect, as straight as a Parthenon pillar. The sight of it restored my courage. It had a tonic effect. Quite unconsciously I pulled my shoulders back and walked ahead with renewed vows never to flinch again. It is a noble tree. It has strength as a giant, and a giant’s height, and yet kindly withal, the branches drooping down graciously towards you﻿—like a kind giant extending its hands to a child.

March 22

A Stagnant Day

===Chinese===

我快步走过，假装没看见，生怕——说到底——连我那本《组织学纲要》也被搅了。到了图书馆里，我又差点让机会溜掉，因为拿起了一本讲化圆为方的书，还被逼着读了它的序言和导论。

3月4日

昆虫学会

到场的"圣甲虫"们济济一堂，彼此展示采集盒里那些钉着针的可怜小虫……这实在是一场独角戏：波尔顿教授在场——一位科学造诣相当可观的人物——用嘶哑的嗓门大喊大叫，准把我们乡间那些胆怯谦和的蝶蛾采集者们吓着了。他像一条威武的牧羊犬，站起来吠一声"孟德尔性状"，或者"种质"，驯顺的羊群便应声聚拢，可怜巴巴地咩咩鼓掌。我想，由于屡屡在这种聚会上听这些和类似的字眼从伟人唇边落下，他们已把它们当作某种仪式的符咒，认为虔诚地照单全收才算恭敬。于是教授每说一声"等位基因"之类，他们便画一个十字，从不敢斗胆问他这到底是个什么鬼。

3月7日

一棵苏格兰冷杉

近来情绪一直很"低"，可昨天我在路边看见一棵挺拔的苏格兰冷杉——高挑，笔直，像帕特农神庙的柱子一样直。一看见它，我的勇气就回来了。它有一剂补药的效验。我全然不自觉地挺起肩膀，大步向前，重新立誓决不再退缩。这是一棵高贵的树。它有力如巨人，有巨人的高度，却又和善可亲，枝条优雅地朝你低垂——像一个和善的巨人向孩子伸出手来。

3月22日

停滞的一天

===Original===

Went to bed late last night so I slept on soundly till 9 a.m. Went down to the bathroom, but found the door was shut, so went back to my bedroom again, lay down and dosed a while, thinking of nothing in particular. Went down again﻿—door still locked﻿—swore﻿—returned once more to my room and reclined on the bed, with door open, so that I could hear as soon as the bathroom door opened.﻿ ﻿… Rang the bell, and Miss —﻿—﻿— brought up a jug of hot water to shave with, and a tumbler of hot water to drink (for my dyspepsia). She, on being interrogated, said there was someone in the bathroom. I said I wanted a bath too, so as she passed on her way down she shouted, “Hurry up, Mr. Barbellion wants a bath as well.” Her footsteps then died away as she descended lower into the basement, where the family lives, sleeps, and cooks our food.

At length, hearing the door open, I ejaculated, “the Lord be praised,” rushed down, entered the bathroom and secured it from further intruders. I observed that Miss —﻿—﻿— senior had been bathing her members, and that the bath, though empty, was covered inside with patches of soap﻿—unutterably black! Oh! Miss —﻿—﻿—!

Dressed leisurely and breakfasted. When the table was cleared wrote a portion of my essay on Spallanzani.﻿ ﻿…

Then, being giddy and tired, rang for dinner. Miss —﻿—﻿— laid the table. She looked very clean. I said, “Good morning,” and she suitably replied, and I went on reading, the *Winning Post*. Felt too slack to be amiable. Next time she came in, I said as pleasantly as I could, “Is it all ready?” and being informed proceeded to eat forthwith.

In the afternoon, took a bus to Richmond. No room outside, so had to go inside﻿—curse﻿—and sit opposite a row﻿—curse again﻿—of fat, ugly, elderly women, all off to visit their married daughters, the usual Sunday jaunt. At Hammersmith got on the outside, and at Turnham Green was caught in a hail storm. Very cold all of a sudden, so got off and took shelter in the doorway of a shop, which was of course closed, the day being Sunday. Rain, wind, and hail continued for some while, as I gazed at the wet, almost empty street, thinking, rethinking and thinking over again the same thought, viz., that the bus ride along this route was exceptionally cheap﻿—probably because of competition with the trams.

===Chinese===

昨夜睡得迟，今早便一觉酣睡到九点。下楼去浴室，门关着，只好回卧室又躺下，迷迷糊糊打了个盹，什么也没特别想。再下楼——门还锁着——骂了一句——再回屋往床上一歪，房门开着，好等浴室门一响我就听见。……按了铃，———小姐端上来一壶刮脸用的热水，还有一杯喝的热水（为我的消化不良）。一问，她说浴室里有人。我说我也要洗澡，于是她下楼时顺口喊了一声："快点，巴贝利恩先生也要洗澡。"随后她的脚步声一路轻下去，消失在地下室——那一家人住着、睡着、给我们做饭的地方。

终于听见门响，我失声一句"感谢主"，冲下楼，进了浴室，闩上门，杜绝再来侵犯者。我看出———大小姐方才洗过她的四肢，浴缸虽空，内壁却结着一层层的皂垢——黑得没法说！哦！———小姐！

不慌不忙穿好衣裳，吃了早饭。桌子收拾掉后，写了一节我那篇论斯帕兰扎尼的文章。……

随后，头昏体乏，摇铃叫饭。———小姐摆桌。她看上去干干净净。我说了声"早安"，她得体地应了一声，我接着读《赛马终点》。懒得客气。她再进来时，我尽量和颜悦色地问："都好了？"得了准信，便立刻开吃。

下午坐公共汽车去里士满。顶层没座，只好坐进车厢里——该死——对面一排——再该死——又胖又丑的中年妇人，全是去看嫁了人的女儿的，礼拜天的例行出游。到哈默史密斯爬上了顶层，到了特纳姆格林又赶上冰雹。忽然冷得很，便下车躲进一家铺子的门洞——礼拜天铺子自然关门。雨、风、雹下了一阵又一阵，我望着湿漉漉、几乎空了的街道，把这个念头想了又想、翻来覆去地想：这条路沿线车票便宜得出奇——大概是跟有轨电车竞争的缘故。

===Original===

The next bus took me to Richmond. Two young girls sat in front, and kept looking back to know if I was “game.” I looked *through* them. Walked in the Park just conscious of the singing of Larks and the chatter of Jays, but harassed mentally by the question, “To whom shall I send my essay, when finished?” To shelter from the rain sat under an oak where four youths joined me and said, “Worse luck,” and “Not half,” and smoked cigarettes. They gossipped and giggled like girls, put their arms around each other’s necks. At the dinner last night, they said, they had Duck and Tomato Soup and Beeswax (“Beesley, you know, the chap that goes about with Smith a lot”) wore a fancy waistcoat with a dinner jacket. When I got up to move on, they became convulsed with laughter. I scowled.

Had tea in the Pagoda tearooms, dry toast and brown bread and butter. Two young men opposite me were quietly playing the fool.

“Hold my hand,” one said audibly enough for two lovers to hear, comfortably settled up in a corner. Even at a side view I could see them kissing each other in between mouthfuls of bread and butter and jam.

On rising to go, one of the two hilarious youths removed my cap and playfully placed it on top of the bowler which his friend was wearing.

“My cap, I think,” I said sharply, and the young man apologised with a splutter. I glared like a killjoy of sixty.

On the bus, coming home, through streets full of motor traffic and all available space plastered with advertisements that screamed at you, I espied in front three pretty girls, who gave me the “Glad Eye.” One had a deep, musical voice, and kept on using it, one of the others a pretty ankle and kept on showing it.

At Kew, two Italians came aboard, one of whom went out of his way to sit among the girls. He sat level with them, and kept turning his head around, giving them a sweeping glance as he did so, to shout remarks in Italian to his friend behind. He thought the girls were prostitutes, I think, and he may have been right. I was on the seat behind this man and for want of anything better to do, studied his face minutely. In short, it was fat, round, and greasy. He wore black moustachios with curly ends, his eyes were dark shining, bulgy, and around his neck was wrapped a scarf inside a dirty linen collar, as if he had a sore throat. I sat behind him and hated him steadily, perseveringly.

===Chinese===

下一班车把我载到里士满。前头坐着两个年轻姑娘，不断回头看我"上不上道"。我把目光*穿透*了她们。在园子里走着，只意识到云雀在唱、松鸦在吵，心里却被一个问题纠缠："文章写完了，该寄给谁？"为躲雨，坐到一棵橡树下，四个小伙子凑过来，说着"真倒霉""可不是嘛"，抽着烟。他们像姑娘们一样叽叽喳喳、咯咯地笑，胳膊搂着彼此的脖子。昨晚那顿饭，他们说，吃了鸭子和番茄汤，还有"比斯蜡"（"就是比斯利，你知道吧，老跟史密斯泡在一块的那个"）穿一件配晚餐服的花哨马甲。我起身要走时，他们笑得抽成一团。我狠狠瞪了一眼。

在宝塔茶室喝了茶，干吐司加黑面包黄油。对面两个年轻人在安静地胡闹。

"握住我的手，"一个说得响亮，足够让角落里安坐的一对恋人听见。即使从侧面我也看得见他们一口黄油果酱面包、一口地互相亲。

起身要走时，两个快活青年中的一个摘下我的便帽，顽皮地扣到他朋友那顶圆顶礼帽顶上。

"这是我的帽子，"我尖利地说，那小伙子结结巴巴道了歉。我像个六十岁的败兴鬼一样怒目而视。

回家的车上，穿过满是汽车的街道，一切空当都糊满了朝你尖叫的广告，我瞥见前头三个漂亮姑娘，朝我抛"媚眼"。一个嗓子低沉悦耳，并且一直用它，另一个脚踝好看，并且一直亮着它。

到了邱园，上来两个意大利人，其中一个特意挤到姑娘们中间坐下。他跟她们坐平，不停回头，一边扫她们一眼，一边用意大利语朝后头的朋友喊话。我想他把姑娘们当成野鸡了，也许他没想错。我坐在这人后面，闲着没事，细细研究他的脸。简而言之：肥、圆、油。他留两撇黑胡子，胡梢打卷，眼睛乌黑发亮、鼓出，脖子上围着围巾，塞在一条脏亚麻领子里，像害了嗓子痛。我坐在他背后，稳稳地、坚持不懈地恨他。

===Original===

At Hammersmith the three girls got off, and the bulgy-eyed Italian watched them go with lascivious eyes, looking over the rail and down at them on the pavement﻿—still interested. I looked down too. They crossed the road in front of us and disappeared.

Came home and here I am writing this. This is the content of today’s consciousness. This is about all I have thought, said, or done, or felt. A stagnant day!

March 26

Home with a bad influenza cold. In a deplorable condition. The best I could do was to sit by the fire and read newspapers one by one from the first page to the last till the reading became mechanical. I found myself reading an account of the Lincoln Handicap and a column article on Kleptomania, while advertisements of new books were devoured with relish as delicacies. My mind became a morass of current Divorce Court News, Society Gossip﻿—“if Sir A. goes Romeward, if Miss B. sings true”﻿—and advertisements. I went on reading because I was afraid to be alone with myself.

B﻿—﻿— arrived at tea and after saying he felt very “pin-eyed” swallowed a glass of Bols gin﻿—the Gin of Antony Bols﻿—and recovered sufficiently to inform me delightedly that he had just won £50. He told me all the story; meanwhile, I, tired of wiping and blowing my nose, sat in the dirty armchair hunched up with elbows on knees and let it drip on to the dirty carpet. B﻿—﻿—, of course, noticed nothing, which was fortunate.

Some kinds of damned fool would have been kindly and sympathetic. I must say I like old B﻿—﻿—. I like him for his simpleness and utter absence of self-consciousness, which make him as charming as a child. Moreover, he often makes me a present of invaluable turf tips. Of course, he is a liar, but his lies are harmless and on his mouth like milk on an infant’s. My own lies are much more dangerous. And when you are ill, to be treated as though you were well is good for hypochondriacs.

April 15

H﻿—﻿—’s wedding. Five minutes before time, I am told I made a dramatic entry into the church clad in an audaciously light pair of Cashmere trousers, lemon-coloured gloves, with top hat and cane. The latter upset the respectability frightfully﻿—it is not *comme il faut*.

April 16

===Chinese===

到了哈默史密斯，三个姑娘下了车，鼓眼的意大利人凭栏俯视着她们在人行道上走远——还饶有兴致。我也低头看。她们从我们车前横过马路，不见了。

回到家，我就在这儿写这篇。这就是今天意识的内容。这差不多就是我今天所想、所说、所做、所感的全部。停滞的一天！

3月26日

在家，重感冒加流感。狼狈不堪。能做的只有坐在炉边，把报纸一份份从头版读到末版，读到机械化。我发现自己读着林肯让赛马的分析和一整栏论盗窃癖的文章，新书广告则被当成美点大嚼特嚼。我的脑子成了一片泥沼，满是最近的离婚法庭新闻、社交界闲话——"要是A爵士投向罗马，要是B小姐唱的是真话"——和广告。我读个不停，因为我不敢和自己单独待着。

B———来喝茶，说罢自己觉得眼睛"针扎似的"，便灌下一杯波尔斯金酒——安东尼·波尔斯家的金酒——缓过劲来，兴冲冲告诉我他刚赢了五十镑。他把全部经过讲给我听；这厢我擦鼻涕擤累了，缩在脏扶手椅里，肘撑着膝，任鼻涕滴到脏地毯上。B———当然什么也没留意，真是万幸。

某种类型的该死的傻瓜此时会又体贴又同情。必须说，我喜欢老B———。我喜欢他的单纯和全然的没有自我意识，这让他像孩子一样可爱。何况他还常送我千金难买的马场内线。当然，他是个撒谎精，可他的谎无害，挂在他嘴上就像奶挂在婴儿嘴上。我自己的谎可危险多了。再说，害病的时候被人当作没病的人对待，对疑病症患者是有好处的。

4月15日

H———的婚礼。据说开席前五分钟，我戏剧性地进入教堂：穿一条胆大包天的浅色羊绒裤，柠檬色手套，配大礼帽和手杖。手杖把体面糟蹋得厉害——实在*不成体统*。

4月16日

===Original===

… If I am to admit the facts they are that I eagerly anticipate love, look everywhere for it, long for it, am unhappy without it. She fascinates me﻿—admitted. I could, if I would, surrender myself. Her affection makes me long to do it. I am sick of living by myself. I am frightened of myself. My life is miserable alone, and sometimes desperately miserable when I long for a little sympathy to be close at hand.

I have often tried to persuade R﻿—﻿— to share a flat with me, because I don’t really wish to marry. I struggle against the idea, I am egotist enough to wish to shirk the responsibilities.

But then I am a ridiculously romantic creature with a wonderful ideal of a woman I shall never meet or if I do she won’t want me﻿—“that (wholly) impossible She.” R﻿—﻿— in a flat with me would partly solve my difficulties. I don’t love her enough for marriage. Mine must be a grand passion, a bouleversement﻿—for I am capable of it.

April 17

A Humble Confession

The Hon. —﻿—﻿—, son and heir of Lord —﻿—﻿—, today invited me to lunch with him in —﻿—﻿— Square. He’s a handsome youth of twenty-five, with fair hair and blue eyes﻿ ﻿… and O! such an aristocrat. Good Lord.

But to continue: the receipt of so unexpected an invitation from so glorious a young gentleman at first gave me palpitation of the heart. I was so surprised that I scarcely had enough presence of mind to listen to the rest of his remarks and later, it was only with the greatest difficulty that I could recall the place where we arranged to meet. His remarks, too, are not easy to follow, as he talks in a stenographic, Alfred-Jingle-like manner, jerking out disjected members of sentences, and leaving you to make the best of them or else to Hell with you﻿—by the Lord, I speak English, don’t I? If I said, “I beg your pardon,” he jerked again, and left me often equally unenlightened.

===Chinese===

……倘若要承认事实，那便是：我热切地期待爱情，到处找它，渴念它，没有它便不快活。她迷住了我——承认。只要我愿意，我可以委身。她的情意叫我想这么做。我厌倦了独自生活。我怕我自己。独居的生活很惨，有时惨到极处，渴望有一点点体贴近在手边。

我常劝R———跟我合住一套公寓，因为我其实并不想结婚。我同这个念头搏斗；我的自我主义够重，想逃避那些责任。

可我又是个可笑的浪漫性子，心里供着一个永远遇不上的完美女子的理想——就算遇上，人家也不会要我——"那位（全然）不可能的她"。R———同我合住，倒能解开我一部分难题。我对她的爱不够做夫妻。我的必须是一场盛大的激情，一场天翻地覆——因为我担当得起。

4月17日

一次谦卑的坦白

尊敬的———先生，———勋爵的独子兼继承人，今天请我去———广场共进午餐。他是个二十五岁的俊俏青年，金发碧眼……哦！好一副贵族气派。老天爷。

接着说：收到这样一位光彩照人的少年绅士如此意外的邀约，起初我竟心悸起来。我惊得几乎不够心神听清他后半截的话，事后费了好大的劲才回想起我们约定的地点。他的话也不好懂，因为他说话像速记，像阿尔弗雷德·金格尔，一句句往外蹦零件，随你自己拼，拼不出就见鬼去——凭主起誓，我说的是英语，不是吗？我说一声"请再说一遍"，他又蹦一串，我还是一样摸不着头脑。

===Original===

On arriving at his home, the first thing he did was to shout down the stairs to the basement: “Elsie, Elsie,” while I gazed with awe at a parcel on the hall table addressed to “Lord —﻿—﻿—.” Before lunch we sat in his little room and talked about —﻿—﻿—, but I was still quite unable to regain my self-composure. I couldn’t for the life of me forget that here was I lunching with Lord —﻿—﻿—’s son, on equal terms, with mutual interests, that his sisters perhaps would come in directly or even the noble Lord himself. I felt like a scared hare. How should I address a peer of the realm? I kept trying to remember and every now and then for some unaccountable reason my mind travelled into —﻿—﻿shire and I saw Auntie C﻿—﻿— serving out tea and sugar over the counter of the baker’s shop in the little village. I luxuriated in the contrast, though I am not at all inclined to be a snob.

He next offered me a cigarette, which I took and lit. It was a Turkish cigarette with one end plugged up with cotton wool﻿—to absorb the nicotine﻿—a, thing I’ve never seen before. I was so flurried at the time that I did not notice this and lit the wrong end. With perfect ease and self-possession, the Honourable One pointed out my error to me and told me to throw the cigarette away and have another.

By this time I had completely lost my nerve. My pride, chagrin, excessive self-consciousness were entangling all my movements in the meshes of a net. Failing to tumble to the situation, I inquired, “Why the *wrong* end? Is there a right and a wrong end?” Lord —﻿—﻿—’s son and heir pointed out the cotton wool end, now blackened by my match.

“That didn’t burn very well, did it?”

I was bound to confess that it did not, and threw the smoke away under the impression that these wonderful cigarettes with right and wrong ends must be some special brand sold only to aristocrats, and at a great price, and possessing some secret virtue. Once again, handsome Mr. —﻿—﻿— drew out his silver cigarette-case, selected a second cigarette for me, and held it towards me between his long delicate fingers, at the same time pointing out the plug at one end and making a few staccato remarks which I could not catch.

===Chinese===

到了他家，他头一件事就是冲着楼梯朝地下室喊："埃尔西，埃尔西！"这厢我诚惶诚恐地盯着门厅桌上一个包裹，收件人是"———勋爵"。午饭前我们坐在他的小房间里谈———，可我仍旧全然镇定不下来。我怎么也忘不掉：我这是在同———勋爵的公子平起平坐地吃午饭，谈得投机，他妹妹们说不定随时进来，甚至勋爵大人本人。我像一只受惊的野兔。我该怎么称呼一位上院贵族？我一遍遍试着记起来，可不知为什么，我的心思时不时飘到———郡，看见C———姨妈在小村面包房铺子柜台后面分发茶叶和糖。我在这份对比里怡然自得，虽然我一点也没有做势利鬼的癖性。

接着他敬我一支烟，我接了，点了。那是一支土耳其烟，一头塞着棉花——用来吸尼古丁——这东西我从前没见过。我当时慌了神，竟没留意，点着了错误的那头。那位贵公子从容不迫、气定神闲地指出我的错处，叫我扔掉，另取一支。

到这时我已完全乱了阵脚。自尊、懊恼、过度的自我意识像一张网，把我的一举一动全缠在网眼里。我没转过弯来，问道："为什么是*错*的一头？难道还分对错两头？"———勋爵的独子兼继承人指了指那截已被我的火柴熏黑的棉花。

"烧得不太好，是吧？"

我只得承认不太好，把烟掐了，心想这种分对错头的神烟，必是只卖给贵族的特别牌子，价钱极贵，且有某种隐秘的功效。俊俏的———先生再次抽出他的银烟盒，给我拣了第二支，用他修长的手指夹着递过来，同时指了指一头塞着的棉花，又迸出几句我没听清的短话。

===Original===

I was still too scared to be in full possession of my faculties, and he apparently was too tired to be explicit to a member of the bourgeoisie, stumbling about his drawing-room. The cotton wool plug only suggested to me some sort of a plot on the part of a dissolute scion of a noble house to lure me into one of his bad habits, such as smoking opium or taking veronal. I again prepared to light the cigarette at the wrong end.

“Try the other end,” repeated the young man, smiling blandly. I blushed, and immediately recovered my balance, and even related my knowledge of pipes fitted to carry similar plugs.﻿ ﻿…

During lunch (at which we sat alone) after sundry visits to the top of the stairs to shout down to the kitchen, he announced that he thought it wasn’t last night’s affair after all which was annoying the Cook (he got home late without a latchkey)﻿—it was because he called her “Cook” instead of Mrs. Austin. He smiled serenely and decided to indulge Mrs. A., his indulgent attitude betraying an objectionable satisfaction with the security of his own unassailable social status. There was a trace of gratification at the little compliment secreted in the Cook’s annoyance. She wanted Mr. Charles to call her Mrs. Austin, forsooth. Very well! and he smiled down on the little weakness *de haute en bas*.

---

===Chinese===

我还是慌得神魂不全，而他也显然懒得对一位资产阶级分子多费唇舌，在客厅里踱来踱去。那截棉花塞只让我疑心，这是没落贵府的一位浪荡子设下的什么圈套，要诱我沾上他的一种恶习，比如抽鸦片或服佛罗拿。我又一次准备点错的那头。

"试试另一头，"青年温文地笑着，又说了一遍。我脸红了，随即恢复平衡，甚至还讲了一通我知道的装着类似塞子的烟斗。……

午饭间（只有我们两人对坐），他几次三番跑到楼梯口朝厨房喊话，然后宣布：他想让厨娘烦心的其实不是昨晚那档子事（他没带门房钥匙，回家晚了）——是因为他叫她"厨娘"，而不叫奥斯汀太太。他安然一笑，决定姑息奥斯汀太太，这份姑息里透出一种叫人讨厌的自得——他那社会地位固若金汤的自得。厨娘的恼火里藏着一句小小的恭维，也有一丝受用的痕迹。她要查尔斯先生叫她奥斯汀太太呢，真的！好得很！他*居高临下*地对着这点小虚荣微笑。

---

===Original===

I enjoyed this little experience. Turning it over in my mind (as the housemaid says when she decides to stay on) I have come to the conclusion that the social parvenu is not such a vulgar fellow after all. He may be a bore﻿—particularly if he sits with his finger tips apposed over a spherical paunch, festooned with a gold chain, and keeps on relating *in extenso* how once he gummed labels on blacking bottles. Often enough he is a smug fellow, yet, truth to tell, we all feel a little interested in him. He is a traveller from an antique land, and we sometimes like to listen to his tales of adventure and all he has come through. He has traversed large territories of human experience, he has met strange folk and lodged in strange caravanserai. Similarly with the man who has come down in the world﻿—the fool, the drunkard, the embezzler﻿—he may bore us with his maudlin sympathy with himself yet his stories hold us. It must be a fine experience within the limits of a single life to traverse the whole keyboard of our social status, whether up or down. I should like to be a peer who grinds a barrel organ or (better still) a onetime organ-grinder who now lives in Park Lane. It must be very dull to remain stationary﻿—once a peer always a peer.

April 20

Miss —﻿—﻿— heard me sigh today and asked what it might mean. “Only the sparks flying upward,” I answered lugubriously.

A blackguard is often unconscious of a good deal of his wickedness. Charge him with wickedness and he will deny it quite honestly﻿—honest then, perhaps, for the first time in his life.

An Entomologist is a large hairy man with eyebrows like antennae.

Chronic constipation has gained for me an unrivalled knowledge of all laxatives, aperients, purgatives and cathartic compounds. At present I arrange two gunpowder plots a week. It’s abominable. Best literature for the latrine: picture puzzles.

April 23

A Foolish Bird

With a menacing politeness, B﻿—﻿— today inquired of a fat curate who was occupying more than his fair share of a seat on top of a bus﻿—

“Are you going to get up or stay where ye are, sir?”

The foolish bird was sitting nearly on top of B﻿—﻿—, mistaking a bomb for an egg.

“I beg your pardon,” replied the fat curate.

B﻿—﻿— repeated his inquiry with more emphasis in the hideous Scotch brogue.

“I suppose I shall stay here till I get down presently.”

===Chinese===

我很享受这段小经历。在脑子里翻来覆去（像女仆决定留下来时说的那样）之后，我得出结论：社会的暴发户毕竟不是那么俗气的一个家伙。他也许叫人腻烦——尤其当他指尖对指尖拢着一个球形大肚皮、肚皮上金链垂花、滔滔不绝地*原原本本*讲他当年往黑鞋油瓶上贴标签的往事时。他常常是个沾沾自喜的家伙，然而说真的，我们对他都有几分兴趣。他是一位从古老国度来的旅人，我们有时爱听他讲他的历险和一路闯过的一切。他穿越过人类经验的广阔疆域，见过奇人，宿过古怪的商队客店。家道中落的人也一样——那傻瓜、酒鬼、挪用公款者——他顾影自怜的醉话也许叫我们烦，可他的故事抓得住我们。在一生一世里，把我们社会地位的全键盘从头到尾走一遍——无论上行还是下行——想必是一趟很好的经历。我倒愿意当一位摇手风琴的勋爵，或者（更好）一位如今住在公园巷的昔日摇琴人。原地不动想必非常乏味——一入贵族终身贵族。

4月20日

———小姐今天听见我叹气，问叹的什么。"不过是火星向上飞罢了，"我凄凄惨惨地答。

恶棍往往意识不到自己许许多多的恶。你指控他作恶，他会全然诚实地否认——那一下的诚实，也许是他生平头一回。

昆虫学家是个毛发浓密的大汉，眉毛长得像触角。

慢性便秘使我赢得了对一切轻泻剂、通便剂、泻药、导泻复方无与伦比的知识。眼下我每周布置两起火药阴谋。真是可恶。厕所最佳读物：拼图。

4月23日

一只蠢鸟

B———今天带着威吓的客气，质问公共汽车顶层一个占了超出应份座位的胖副牧师——

"您是打算起来呢，还是就待在那儿，先生？"

这只蠢鸟几乎坐在B———身上，把炸弹错当成了鸡蛋。

"请您再说一遍？"胖副牧师答。

B———用更重的苏格兰土腔把问话重复了一遍。

"我想我就待在这儿，等会儿再下去吧。"

===Original===

“I don’t think you will,” said B﻿—﻿—.

“What do you mean?” asked the fat one in falsetto indignation.

“This,” B﻿—﻿— grunted, and shunted sideways so that the poor fellow almost slid on to the floor.

---

A posse of police walking along in single file always makes me laugh. A single constable is a Policeman, but several in single file are “Coppers.” I imagine everyone laughs at them and I have a shrewd suspicion it is one of W. S. Gilbert’s legacies﻿—the *Pirates of Penzance* having become part of the national Consciousness.

On Lighting Chloe’s Cigarette

R﻿—﻿— remarked today that he intended writing a lyric on lighting Chloe’s cigarette.

“Ah!” I said at once appreciative, “now tell me, do you balance your hand﻿—by gently (ever so gently) resting the extreme tip of your little finger upon her chin, and” (I was warming up) “do you hold the match vertically or horizontally, and do you light it in the dark or in the light? If you have finesse, you won’t need to be told that the thing is to get a steady flame and the maximum of illumination upon her face to last over a period for as long as possible.”

“Chloe,” replied R﻿—﻿—, “is wearing now a charming blouse with a charming V-shaped opening in front. Her Aunt asked my Mother last night tentatively, ‘How do you like Chloe’s blouse? Is it too low?’ My Mother scrutinised the dear little furry, lop-eared thing and answered doubtfully, ‘No, Maria, I don’t think so.’ ”

“How ridiculous! Why the V is a positive signpost. My dear fellow,” I said to R﻿—﻿—, “I should refuse to be bluffed by those old women. Tell them you *know*.”

Carlyle called Lamb a despicable abortion. What a crime!

May 2

===Chinese===

"我看您待不成，"B———说。

"您什么意思？"胖子用假嗓怒问。

"这个意思，"B———哼了一声，身子往旁一拱，可怜人几乎滑到地板上。

---

一队警察鱼贯而行，总能让我发笑。单独一名警察是"警察先生"，成串的几个便是"条子"。我想人人见他们都要笑，我精明地疑心这是W. S. 吉尔伯特的遗产之一——《彭赞斯的海盗》已成了民族意识的一部分。

论给克洛伊点烟

R———今天说，他打算写一首给克洛伊点烟的抒情诗。

"啊！"我立刻心领神会，"那您说说，您是不是把手稳住——用小指的指尖（轻轻地、极轻极轻地）搭在她的下巴上，还有，"（我渐渐来劲）"火柴您是竖着拿还是横着拿？是在暗处点还是亮处点？您若有雅趣，不必人教也知道：诀窍是要一苗稳火，让照在她脸上的光亮到最大，并且撑得越久越好。"

"克洛伊，"R———答道，"如今穿一件迷人的短衫，前头开着一道迷人的V字形。她姑妈昨晚试探着问我母亲：'你看克洛伊这件上衣怎么样？领口是不是太低了？'我母亲把那件亲爱的小毛毛、耷拉耳朵的东西端详了一番，迟疑地答：'不低，玛丽亚，我看不低。'"

"多可笑！那V字分明是一块路牌。亲爱的伙计，"我对R———说，"你可别被那些老太婆唬住。告诉她们，你*心里有数*。"

卡莱尔骂兰姆是个卑劣的怪胎。多大的罪过！

5月2日

===Original===

Developed a savage fit. Up to a certain point, perhaps, but beyond that anxiety changes into recklessness﻿—you simply don’t care. The aperients are causing dyspepsia and intermittent action of the heart, which frightens me. After a terrifying week, during which at crises I have felt like dropping suddenly in the street, in the gardens, anywhere, from syncope, I rebelled against this humiliating fear. I pulled my shoulders back and walked briskly ahead along the street with a dropped beat every two or three steps. I laughed bitterly at it and felt it could stop or go on﻿—I was at last indifferent. In a photographer’s shop was the picture of a very beautiful woman and I stopped to look at her. I glowered in through the glass angrily and reflected how she was gazing out with that same expression even at the butcher’s boy or the lamplighter. It embittered me to think of having to leave her to some other man. To me she represented all the joy of life which at any moment I might have had to quit forever. Such impotence enraged me and I walked off up the street with a whirling heart and the thought, “I shall drop, I suppose, when I get up as far as that.” Yet don’t think I was alarmed. Oh! no. The iron had entered me, and I went on with cynical indifference waiting to be struck down.

… She is a very great deal to me. Perhaps I love her very much after all.

May 3

Bad heart attack all day. Intermittency is very refined torture to one who wants to live very badly. Your pump goes a “dot and carry one,” or say “misses a stitch,” what time you breathe deep, begin to shake your friend’s hand and make a farewell speech. Then it goes on again and you order another pint of beer.

It is a fractious animal within the cage of my thorax, and I never know when it is going to escape and make off with my precious life between its teeth. I humour and coax and soothe it, but, God wot, I haven’t much confidence in the little beast. My thorax it appears is an intolerable kennel.

May 10

In a very cheerful mood. Pleased with myself and everybody till a seagull soared overhead in Kensington Gardens and aroused my vast capacities for envy﻿—I wish I could fly.

May 24

===Chinese===

发了一场凶悍的病。到某个限度为止，焦虑也许还有；过了那个限度，焦虑就变成了孤注一掷——你干脆什么都不在乎了。泻药害得我消化不良、心脏间歇，这叫我害怕。熬过可怕的一星期——危象时刻，我觉得自己随时会在街上、花园里、随便哪里因昏厥突然倒下——之后，我反了这份丢人的怕。我挺起肩膀，沿街大步向前，每走两三步漏跳一拍。我朝它苦笑，觉得它爱停就停、爱跳就跳——我终于无所谓了。一家照相馆橱窗里挂着一位非常美丽的女子的相片，我停下来看她。我隔着玻璃怒视，心想她这副神情连肉铺小伙计、点灯人路过时也照看不误。想到不得不把她留给别的男人，我心里发苦。于我，她代表着人生的全部欢乐，而我随时可能从此永别。这份无能激怒了我，我心头翻滚着走上街去，想着："走到那边那处，大概我就倒下了。"可别以为我惊慌。哦！不。铁已经进了我的骨头，我怀着愤世的冷漠继续走，等着被击倒。

……她于我意义重大。说到底，也许我非常爱她。

5月3日

整日心脏病发作。对想活到极处的人，间歇是一种精致的酷刑。你那台泵"一颠一簸"地走，或者说"漏一针"，这厢你深吸一口气，开始握朋友的手，发表诀别的演说。随后它又走起来，你便再叫一品脱啤酒。

它是关在我胸腔这只笼子里的一头倨傲牲口，我从不知道它几时会挣脱，把我宝贵的性命衔在嘴里跑掉。我哄它、抚它、劝它，可是天知道，我对这小畜生没有多少信心。我这胸腔，看来是一只叫它忍无可忍的狗窝。

5月10日

情绪非常之好。对自己对人人得意，直到一只海鸥在肯辛顿园上空翱翔，勾起我巨大的艳羡本事——但愿我也会飞。

5月24日

===Original===

In L﻿—﻿— with my brother, A﻿—﻿—. The great man is in great form and very happy in his love for N﻿—﻿—. He is a most delightful creature and I love him more than anyone else in the wide world. There is an almost feminine tenderness in my love.

We spent a delightful day, talking and arguing and insulting one another.﻿ ﻿… At these séances we take delight in anaesthetising our hearts for the purposes of argument, and a third person would be bound to suppose we were in the throes of a bitter quarrel. We pile up one vindictive remark on another, ingeniously seeking out﻿—and with malice﻿—weak points in each other’s armour, which previous exchange of confidences makes it easy to find. Neither of us hesitates to make use of such private confessions, yet our love is so strong that we can afford to take any liberty. There is, in fact, a fearful joy in testing the strength of our affection by searching for cutting rejoinders﻿—to see the effect. We rig up one another’s cherished ideals like Aunt Sallies and then knock them down, we wax sarcastic, satirical, contemptuous in turn, we wave our hands animatedly (hand-waving is a great trick with both of us), get flushed, point with our fingers, and thump the table to clinch some bit of repartee. Yet it’s all smoke. Our love is unassailable﻿—it’s like the law of gravitation, you cannot dispute it, it underlies our existence, it is the air we breathe.

N﻿—﻿— is charming, and thought we were quarrelling, and therefore intervened on his side!

May 31

R﻿—﻿— outlined an impression he had in Naples one day during a sirocco of the imminence of his own death. It was evidently an isolated experience and bored me a little as I could have said a lot myself about that. When he finished I drew from my pocket an envelope with my name and three addresses scribbled on it to help the police in case of syncope as I explained. I have carried this with me for several years and at one time a flask of brandy.

June 3

Went to see the Irish Players in *The Playboy*. Sitting in front of me was a charming little Irish girl accompanied by a male clod with red-rimmed eyes like a Bull-terrier’s, a sandy, bristly moustache like a housemaid’s broom, and a face like a gluteal mass, and a horrid voice that crepitated rather than spoke.

===Chinese===

在L———同兄弟A———一起。这位伟人状态极佳，同N———的恋爱谈得很快活。他是顶叫人喜欢的人，普天之下我爱他胜过任何人。我的爱里有一种近乎女性的温柔。

我们快快活活过了一天，交谈、争论、彼此辱骂。……在这样的降神会上，我们乐于把心麻醉了来吵架，第三者准以为我们正吵得天昏地黑。我们恶语一句压一句，存心——并且带着恶意——找对方铠甲上的破绽，先前交换过的隐秘使这破绽一找一个准。我们俩谁都不吝动用那些私房忏悔，可我们的爱结实得很，禁得起任何放肆。事实上，用寻章摘句的毒话去试爱的斤两、看看效果，有一种可怕的快感。我们把彼此心爱的理想竖起来当打靶的玩偶，再一枪打翻；我们轮流挖苦、讥刺、鄙夷；我们起劲地挥手（挥手是我们俩的一大绝技）；我们涨红了脸，用手指点着，拍桌子给某句妙语定音。可这全是烟。我们的爱攻不破——像万有引力，你无法同它争辩，它垫在我们的存在底下，是我们呼吸的空气。

N———可爱得很，以为我们真吵架了，还站到了他那边！

5月31日

R———讲了他有朝一日在那不勒斯遇上一场西罗科风时，忽觉死亡将至的印象。那显然是一桩孤立的体验，我听得有点腻，因为这话题我自己能讲一大筐。他讲完，我从口袋里掏出一只信封，上面潦草写着我的名字和三个地址——我解释说，是备昏厥时帮警察认人的。这信封我揣了好几年了，有一阵还揣着一小瓶白兰地。

6月3日

去看爱尔兰剧团演《西方世界的花花公子》。我前排坐着个迷人的爱尔兰小姑娘，身边陪着个粗坯男人：眼圈红得像牛头梗，沙色的硬髭像女管家的扫帚，一张脸活像一坨臀肉，嗓子破锣似的，与其说说话不如说噼啪作响。

===Original===

She was dark, with shining blue eyes, and a delightful little nose of the utmost import to every male who should gaze upon her. Between the acts, the clod hearkened to her vivacious conversation﻿—like an enchanted bullock. Her vivacity was such that the tip of her nose moved up and down for emphasis and by the end of the Third Act I was captured entirely. Lucky dog, that clod!

After the play this little Irish maiden caught my eye and it became a physical impossibility for me to check a smile﻿—and oh! Heavens!﻿—she gave me a smile in return. Precisely five seconds later, she looked again to see if I was still smiling﻿—I was﻿—and we then smiled broadly and openly on one another﻿—her smile being the timorous ingénue’s not the glad eye of a *femme de joie*. Later, on the railway platform whither I followed her, I caught her eye again (was ever so lucky a fellow?), and we got into the same carriage. But so did the clod﻿—ah! dear, was ever so unlucky a fellow? Forced to occupy a seat some way off, but she caught me trying to see her through a midnight forest of opera hats, lace ruffles, projecting ears and fat noses.

Curse! Left her at High Street Station and probably will never see her again. This is a second great opportunity. The first was the girl on Lundy Island. These two women I shall always regret. There must be so many delightful and interesting persons in London if only I could get at them.

June 4

Rushed off to tell R﻿—﻿— about my little Irish girl. Her face has been “shadowing” me all day.

June 6

A violent argument with R﻿—﻿— re marriage. He says Love means appropriation, and is taking the most elaborate precautions to forfend passion﻿—just as if it were a militant suffragette. Every woman he meets he first puts into a long quarantine, lest perchance she carries the germ of the infectious disease. He quotes Hippolytus and talks like a medieval ascetic. Himself, I imagine, he regards as a valuable but brittle piece of Dresden china which must be saved from rough handling and left unmolested to pursue its high and dusty destiny﻿—an old crock as I warned him. By refusing to plunge into life he will live long and be a well preserved man, but scarcely a living man﻿—a mummy rather. I told him so amid much laughter.

“You’re a reactionary,” says he.

“Yes, but why should a reactionary be a naughty boy?”

June 7

===Chinese===

她皮肤深色，眼睛蓝得发亮，那只可爱的小鼻子，对每一个看她一眼的男人都有莫大的干系。幕间，那粗坯听她活泼的谈话——像一头中了魔的牛。她活泼到鼻尖都随语气一翘一落，到第三幕结束，我已全军覆没。那粗坯真是幸运的畜生！

散场后，这位爱尔兰小姑娘同我目光相接，我实在忍不住一个微笑——哦！天哪！——她也回了我一个微笑。整整五秒后，她又看过来，瞧我是不是还在笑——我在——于是我们彼此敞开大笑了——她的笑是羞怯天真少女的笑，不是*风尘女子*抛的媚眼。随后在站台上（我跟了她去），我又接住了她的目光（谁曾这样走运？），我们上了同一节车厢。可那粗坯也上了——啊！天哪，谁曾这样倒霉？我只好坐在隔几排的地方，可她还是发现我正从午夜的礼帽、蕾丝花边、招风耳和肥鼻子的森林里努力看她。

该死！在海街车站同她分手，多半再也见不着了。这是第二个大好机会。头一个是兰迪岛上的那个姑娘。这两个女人我将永远引以为憾。伦敦一定还有多少可爱有趣的人，只恨我够不着她们。

6月4日

跑去把这爱尔兰小姑娘讲给R———听。她的脸整日"附"在我身上。

6月6日

同R———为婚姻大吵一场。他说爱就是占有，并正以最周密的预防措施抵御激情——仿佛激情是个激进的妇女参政论者。他遇到的每个女人，先送进长期检疫，生怕她身上带着这种传染病的病菌。他引《希波吕托斯》，说话像中世纪的苦行僧。他自己，我想，被他当作一件贵重而易碎的德累斯顿瓷，必须保全它不受粗手粗脚，任它清清爽爽去走它那条高贵而蒙尘的命运之路——一件老古董，我如此警告他。他拒绝投身生活，将会活得长久，保存完好，却算不上一个活人——毋宁说是一具木乃伊。我在大笑中把这番话告诉了他。

"你是个反动派，"他说。

"是，可反动派为什么就得是淘气孩子？"

6月7日

===Original===

My ironical fate lured me this evening into another discussion on marriage in which I had to take up a position exactly opposite to the one I defended yesterday against R﻿—﻿—. In fact, I actually subverted to my own pressing requirements some of R﻿—﻿—’s own arguments! The argument, of course, was with Her.

Marriage, I urged, was an economic trap for guileless young men, and for my part (to give myself some necessary stiffening) I did not intend to enter upon any such hazardous course, even if I had the chance. Miss —﻿—﻿— said I was a funk﻿—to me who the day before had been hammering into R﻿—﻿— my principle of “Plunge and damn the consequences.” I was informed I was an old woman afraid to go out without an umbrella, an old tabby cat afraid to leave the kitchen fire, etc., etc.

“Yes, I *am* afraid to go out without an umbrella,” I argued formally, “when it’s raining cats and dogs. As long as I am dry, I shall keep dry. As soon as I find myself caught in the rain or victimised by a passion, I shan’t be afraid of falling in love or getting wet. It would be a misadventure, but I am not going in search of one.”

All the same the discussion was very galling, for I was acting a part.

… The truth is I have philandered abominably with her. I know it. And now I am jibbing at the idea of marriage.﻿ ﻿… I am such an egotist, I want, I believe, a Princess of the Blood Royal.

June 9

Some days ago sent a personal advertisement to the newspaper to try to find my little Irish girl who lives at Notting Hill Gate. Today they return me the money and advert, no doubt mistaking me for a White Slave trafficker. And by this time, I’m thinking, my little Irish girl can go to blazes. Shall spend the P.O. on sweets or monkey nuts.

June 10

Lupus

===Chinese===

我那爱嘲弄的命运，今晚又把我诱进一场关于婚姻的争论，我只好站在昨天痛驳R———的那个立场的正对面。实际上，我竟把R———的论点挪过来，凑了我眼下的急用！争论的对象，自然是"她"。

我振振有词：婚姻是给天真青年设下的经济陷阱，而我（为给自己打气）压根不打算踏进这等险途，哪怕有机会。———小姐说我是个胆小鬼——对我这个前一天还在向R———敲打"只管跳，后果见鬼去"信条的人。又说我是个出门不带伞就害怕的老太婆，一只不敢离开灶火的老公猫，等等，等等。

"不错，倾盆大雨的时候，我*是*不敢不带伞出门，"我一本正经地辩，"只要我干着，我就要一直干着。一旦真落了雨、或者真害了激情，我就不怕恋爱、不怕淋湿了。那是飞来横祸，可我不去自找。"

这场讨论终究叫人窝火，因为我演的是戏。

……实情是，我同她调情调得不像话。我知道。如今我却在婚姻门槛前畏缩。……我这自我主义者，我相信，我要的是一位金枝玉叶的公主。

6月9日

几天前往报上登了一则寻人启事，想找那位住在诺丁山门的小爱尔兰姑娘。今天报社把钱和广告退了回来，八成把我当成了拐卖白奴的贩子。到这会儿，我想，我的小爱尔兰姑娘可以见鬼去了。这笔退回的汇票钱，拿去买糖或者花生米吧。

6月10日

狼疮

===Original===

It is raining heavily. I have just finished dinner. In the street an itinerant musician is singing dolefully, “O Rest in the Lord.” In my dirty little sitting room I begin to feel very restless, so put on my hat and cloak and walk down towards the Station for a paper to read. It is all very dark and dismal, and I gaze with hungry eyes in through some of the windows disclosing happy comfortable interiors. At intervals thunder growls and lightning brightens up the deserted dirtiness of the Station Waiting Room. A few bits of desolate paper lie about on the floor, and up in one corner on a form a crossing-sweeper, motionless and abject, driven in from his pitch by the rain. His hands are deep in his trousers’ pockets, and the poor devil lies with legs sprawling out and eyes closed: over the lower part of his face he wears a black mask to hide the ravages of lupus.﻿ ﻿… He seemed the last man on earth﻿—after everyone else had died of the plague. Not a soul in the station. Not a train. And this is June!

June 15

Measuring Lice

Spent the day measuring the legs and antennae of lice to two places of decimals!

To the lay mind how fantastic this must seem! Indeed, I hope it is fantastic. I do not mind being thought odd. It seems almost fitting that an incurable dilettante like myself should earn his livelihood by measuring the legs of lice. I like to believe that such a bizarre manner of life suits my incurable frivolousness.

I am a Magpie in a Baghdad bazaar, hopping about, useless, inquisitive, fascinated by a lot of astonishing things: e.g., a book on the quadrature of the circle, the gabbertushed fustilugs passage in Burton’s *Anatomy of Melancholy*, names like Mr. Portwine or Mr. Hogsflesh, Tweezer’s Alley or Pickle Herring Street, the excellent, conceitful sonnets of Henry Constable or Petticoat Lane on a Sunday morning.

Colossal things such as Art, Science, etc., frighten me. I am afraid I should develop a thirst that would make me wish to drink the sea dry. My mind is a disordered miscellany. The world is too distracting. I cannot apply myself for long. London bewilders me. At times it is a phantasmagoria, an opium dream out of De Quincey.

June 17

===Chinese===

大雨滂沱。我刚吃完晚饭。街上一个卖唱的艺人凄凄惨惨地唱着《安息于主》。在我这间又脏又小的起居室里，我开始烦躁起来，于是戴上帽子披上斗篷，朝车站走去，买份报看。四下昏暗凄凉，我隔着几扇亮出温暖舒适内景的窗户，用饥饿的眼睛张望。雷声一阵阵滚，闪电一阵阵照亮候车室里无人问津的脏乱。地板上散着几片纸屑，角落一条长凳上，一个扫街的，被雨赶离了他的街口，一动不动、委顿不堪地躺着。他双手深插在裤袋里，可怜虫两腿摊开，闭着眼：脸的下半部罩着一个黑口罩，遮着狼疮啃过的痕迹。……他像是瘟疫过后世上剩下的最后一个人。车站里一个人影也没有。一班车也没有。而这时是六月！

6月15日

量虱子

这一整天都花在把虱子的腿和触角量到小数点后两位！

在外行人听来这多么匪夷所思！确实，我巴不得它匪夷所思。我不怕人家当我怪人。像我这样一个无可救药的杂家，靠量虱子腿谋生，几乎可说是得其所哉。我乐于相信，这种古怪的谋生方式正配我无可救药的轻浮。

我是巴格达集市上的一只喜鹊，蹦来跳去，百无一用，好奇心重，被一大堆惊人的东西迷住：比如一本论化圆为方的书，伯顿《忧郁的解剖》里那段古怪字眼的妙文，波特万先生或霍格斯弗莱希先生这样的名字，镊子巷或腌鲱鱼街，亨利·康斯特布尔的那些妙想连篇的十四行诗，或者礼拜天早晨的衬裙巷。

艺术、科学这一类庞然大物吓着我。我怕自己会渴得想把大海喝干。我的头脑是一团乱七八糟的大杂烩。这世界岔人的东西太多。我干什么都长久不了。伦敦叫我晕头转向。有时它是一场幻影集，一出德·昆西的鸦片梦。

6月17日

===Original===

Prof. Geo. Saintsbury’s book on Elizabethan literature amuses me. *George*, there can be no doubt, is a very refined, cultivated fellow. I bet he don’t eat periwinkles with a pin or bite his nails﻿—and you should hear him refer to folk who can’t read Homer in the original or who haven’t been to Oxford﻿—to Merton above all. He also says *non so che* for *je ne sais quoi*.

June 26

… I placed the volume on the mantelpiece as if it were a bottle of physic straight from my Dispensary, and I began to expostulate and expound, as if she were a sick person and I the doctor.﻿ ﻿… She seemed a little nettled at my proselytising demeanour and gave herself out to be very preoccupied﻿—or at any rate quite uninterested in my physic. I read the book last night at one sitting and was boiling over with it.

“I fear I have come at an inconvenient time,” I said, with a sardonic smile and strummed on the piano.﻿ ﻿… “I must really be off. Please read it (which sounded like ‘three times a day after meals’) and tell me how you like it. (Facetiously.) Of course don’t give up your present manual for it, that would be foolish and unnecessary.”﻿ ﻿… I rambled on﻿—disposed to be very playful.

At last calmly and horribly, in a thoughtful voice she answered﻿—

“I think you are very rude; you play the piano after I asked you to stop and walk about just as if it were your own home.”

I remained outwardly calm but inwardly was very surprised and full of tremors. I said after a pause﻿—

“Very well, if you think so.﻿ ﻿… Goodbye.”

No answer; and I was too proud to apologise.

“Goodbye,” I repeated.

She went on reading her novel in silence while I got as far as the door﻿—very upset.

“*Au revoir.*”

No answer.

“Oh,” said I, and went out of the room leaving my lady for good and all and I’m not sorry.

In the passage met Miss —﻿—﻿—. “What?” she said, “going already?”

“Farewell,” I said sepulchrally. “A very tragic farewell,” which left her wondering.

June 29

At the Albert Hall

Went with R﻿—﻿— to the Albert Hall to the *Empress of Ireland* Memorial Concert with massed bands. We heard the *Symphonie Pathétique*, Chopin’s Funeral March, “Trauermarsch” from *Götterdammerung*, the “Ride of the Valkyries” and a solemn melody from Bach.

===Chinese===

圣茨伯里教授论伊丽莎白时代文学的书把我逗乐了。*乔治*，毫无疑问，是个非常精致、有教养的家伙。我打赌他绝不用大头针挑海螺吃，也不啃指甲——你真该听听他怎么说那些读不懂希腊文原版荷马、或者没上过牛津——尤其是没上过默顿学院——的人。他还拿意大利语的*non so che*来顶法语的*je ne sais quoi*。

6月26日

……我把那卷书往壁炉台上一放，仿佛它是药房刚送来的一瓶药水，然后开始规劝、开导，仿佛她是病人，我是医生。……她似乎被我这份布道派头惹恼了一点，自称忙得很——或者说，对我的药水毫无兴趣。那书我昨晚一口气读完，正沸腾着。

"我怕我来得不巧，"我说，带着讥诮的笑，随手在钢琴上乱弹。……"我真该走了。请读读它（听上去像'每日三次，饭后服'），再告诉我你喜欢不喜欢。（玩笑地。）当然别为它丢了手头的教科书，那是愚蠢而不必要的。"……我东拉西扯——一心想顽皮一场。

终于，她平静而可怕地、若有所思地答道——

"我觉得你很没规矩；我请你别弹了你还弹，还在屋里走来走去，就像这是你自己家。"

我外表平静，心里却大为惊愕，抖个不住。顿了一顿，我说——

"很好，你既然这么想。……再见。"

没有回答；我又骄傲得不肯道歉。

"再见，"我又说了一遍。

她默默读着小说，我一路走到了门口——心里翻江倒海。

"*回见*。"

没有回答。

"哦，"我说了一句，走出房门，从此撇下了我的夫人，我并不惋惜。

过道里碰见———小姐。"怎么？"她说，"这就走？"

"永别了，"我墓穴般庄严地说，"一出非常悲惨的永别，"叫她怔了半晌。

6月29日

在皇家阿尔伯特音乐厅

同R———去阿尔伯特音乐厅，看《爱尔兰皇后号》纪念音乐会，军乐联合演出。我们听了《悲怆交响曲》、肖邦的《葬礼进行曲》、《众神的黄昏》里的"送葬进行曲"、"女武神的骑行"，还有巴赫一支庄严的旋律。

===Original===

This afternoon I regard as a mountain peak in my existence. For two solid hours I sat like an Eagle on a rock gazing into infinity﻿—a very fine sensation for a London Sparrow.﻿ ﻿…

I have an idea that if it were possible to assemble the sick and suffering day by day in the Albert Hall and keep the Orchestra going all the time, then the constant exposure of sick parts to such heavenly air vibrations would ultimately restore to them the lost rhythm of health. Surely, even a single exposure to﻿—say Beethoven’s Fifth Symphony﻿—must result in some permanent reconstitution of ourselves body and soul. No one can be quite the same after a Beethoven Symphony has streamed through him.

If one could *develop* a human soul like a negative the effect I should say could be seen.﻿ ﻿… I’ll tell you what I wish they’d do﻿—seriously: divide up the arena into a series of cubicles where, unobserved and in perfect privacy, a man could execute all the various movements of his body and limbs which the music prompts. It would be such a delicious self-indulgence and it’s torture to be jammed into a seat where you can’t even tap one foot or wave an arm.

The concert restored my moral health. I came away in love with people I was hating before and full of compassion for others I usually condemn. A feeling of immeasurable well being﻿—a jolly bonhomie enveloped me like incandescent light. At the close when we stood up to sing the National Anthem we all felt a genuine spirit of camaraderie. Just as when Kings die, we were silent musing upon the common fate, and when the time came to separate we were loath to go our several ways, for we were comrades who together had come through a great experience.
